  /// Returns `ConfigError` when reading or (de)serializing fails.\n   151→    pub fn from_sources<P: AsRef<Path>>(\n   152→        base_path: P,\n   153→        env_prefix: &str,\n   154→    ) -> Result<Self, ConfigError> {\n   155→        let mut builder = Config::builder();\n   156→\n   157→        // --- 1. This module demonstrates the Repository pattern, a thin Service layer,\n//! domain-event emission, and secure session management (JWT + Redis).\n//! It is self-contained and can be dropped into the larger workspace.\n//!\n//! Dependencies expected in `Cargo.toml` (not shown here):\n//! --------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! failure scenarios.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum AuthError {\n    #[error(\"hashing error\")]\n    Hashing(#[from] argon2::password_hash::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"database error\")]\n    Database(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"invalid credentials\")]\n    InvalidCredentials,\n\n    #[error(\"jwt error\")]\n    Jwt(#[from] jsonwebtoken::errors::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"event bus error: {0}\")]\n    EventBus(#[from] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Domain model\n/// =============================================================================================\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct User {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub email: String,\n    #[serde(skip_serializing)]\n    pub password_hash: String,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\npub struct NewUser {\n    pub email: String,\n    pub password: String,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct Session {\n    pub token: String,\n    pub expires_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// JWT claims stored in the access token\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\nstruct Claims {\n    sub: String,\n    exp: usize,\n    iat: usize,\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Repository layer\n/// =============================================================================================\n#[async_trait]\npub trait UserRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn insert(&self, user: &User) -> Result<()>;\n    async fn find_by_email(&self, email: &str) -> Result<Option<User>>;\n    async fn find_by_id(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<Option<User>>;\n}\n\npub struct PostgresUserRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PostgresUserRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl UserRepository for PostgresUserRepository {\n    async fn insert(&self, user: &User) -> Result<()> {\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO users (id, email, password_hash, created_at)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(user.id)\n        .bind(&user.email)\n        .bind(&user.password_hash)\n        .bind(user.created_at)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn find_by_email(&self, email: &str) -> Result<Option<User>> {\n        let maybe_row = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, email, password_hash, created_at\n            FROM users\n            WHERE email = $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(email)\n        .map(map_row_to_user)\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(maybe_row)\n    }\n\n    async fn find_by_id(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<Option<User>> {\n        let maybe_row = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, email, password_hash, created_at\n            FROM users\n            WHERE id = $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(id)\n        .map(map_row_to_user)\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(maybe_row)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Helper for mapping a row into a [`User`].\nfn map_row_to_user(row: PgRow) -> User {\n    User {\n        id: row.get(\"id\"),\n        email: row.get(\"email\"),\n        password_hash: row.get(\"password_hash\"),\n        created_at: row.get(\"created_at\"),\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Event bus abstraction\n/// =============================================================================================\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    UserRegistered { user_id: Uuid, email: String },\n    UserLoggedIn { user_id: Uuid },\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventBus: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, evt: &DomainEvent) -> anyhow::Result<()>;\n}\n\n/// A naive, logging-only event bus implementation. Replace with Kafka/NATS/RabbitMQ as needed.\npub struct TracingEventBus;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventBus for TracingEventBus {\n    async fn publish(&self, evt: &DomainEvent) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        info!(?evt, \"domain event published\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Session cache (Redis)\n/// =============================================================================================\nuse deadpool_redis::{cmd, redis::AsyncCommands, Config, Connection, Pool};\n\npub struct SessionStore {\n    pool: Pool,\n}\n\nimpl SessionStore {\n    pub fn new(redis_url: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {\n        let mut cfg = Config::from_url(redis_url);\n        cfg.pool.timeout = Some(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));\n\n        let pool = cfg.create_pool(Some(deadpool_redis::Runtime::Tokio1))?;\n        Ok(Self { pool })\n    }\n\n    async fn put(&self, token: &str, user_id: Uuid, ttl: Duration) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        cmd(\"SET\")\n            .arg(token)\n            .arg(user_id.to_string())\n            .arg(\"EX\")\n            .arg(ttl.num_seconds())\n            .query_async(&mut conn)\n            .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    async fn get_user_id(&self, token: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Option<Uuid>> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        let resp: Option<String> = conn.get(token).await?;\n        Ok(resp.and_then(|s| Uuid::parse_str(&s).ok()))\n    }\n\n    async fn delete(&self, token: &str) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n        let mut conn: Connection = self.pool.get().await?;\n        conn.del(token).await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// Service layer\n/// =============================================================================================\npub struct AuthService<R: UserRepository, B: EventBus> {\n    repo: R,\n    event_bus: B,\n    session_store: SessionStore,\n    jwt_secret: String,\n    session_ttl: Duration,\n    argon2: Argon2<'static>,\n}\n\nimpl<R: UserRepository, B: EventBus> AuthService<R, B> {\n    /// Create an [`AuthService`] with sensible defaults.  \n    /// `jwt_secret` **must** be a strong, randomly-generated 256-bit key (base64 encoded).\n    pub fn new(repo: R, event_bus: B, session_store: SessionStore, jwt_secret: String) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            event_bus,\n            session_store,\n            jwt_secret,\n            session_ttl: Duration::hours(12),\n            argon2: Argon2::default(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Registers a new user, returning a newly minted [`Session`] on success.\n    pub async fn register_user(&self, new_user: NewUser) -> Result<Session> {\n        if self.repo.find_by_email(&new_user.email).await?.is_some() {\n            return Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials); // simplified: email already taken\n        }\n\n        let salt = SaltString::generate(&mut rand::thread_rng());\n        let password_hash = self\n            .argon2\n            .hash_password(new_user.password.as_bytes(), &salt)?\n            .to_string();\n\n        let user = User {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            email: new_user.email,\n            password_hash,\n            created_at: Utc::now(),\n        };\n\n        self.repo.insert(&user).await?;\n        self.event_bus\n            .publish(&DomainEvent::UserRegistered {\n                user_id: user.id,\n                email: user.email.clone(),\n            })\n            .await?;\n\n        let session = self.create_session(user.id).await?;\n        Ok(session)\n    }\n\n    /// Authenticates a user and returns a valid session token.\n    pub async fn login_user(&self, email: &str, password: &str) -> Result<Session> {\n        let user = self\n            .repo\n            .find_by_email(email)\n            .await?\n            .ok_or(AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n        // Verify password\n        let parsed_hash = PasswordHash::new(&user.password_hash)?;\n        self.argon2\n            .verify_password(password.as_bytes(), &parsed_hash)\n            .map_err(|_| AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n        let session = self.create_session(user.id).await?;\n\n        self.event_bus\n            .publish(&DomainEvent::UserLoggedIn { user_id: user.id })\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(session)\n    }\n\n    /// Invalidates an existing session.\n    pub async fn logout_user(&self, token: &str) -> Result<()> {\n        self.session_store\n            .delete(token)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| AuthError::EventBus(e.into()))?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Helper: create a JWT token + Redis cache entry.\n    async fn create_session(&self, user_id: Uuid) -> Result<Session> {\n        let now = Utc::now();\n        let exp = (now + self.session_ttl).timestamp() as usize;\n\n        let claims = Claims {\n            sub: user_id.to_string(),\n            exp,\n            iat: now.timestamp() as usize,\n        };\n\n        let header = Header::default();\n        let token = encode(\n            &header,\n            &claims,\n            &EncodingKey::from_secret(self.jwt_secret.as_bytes()),\n        )?;\n\n        // Cache in Redis for server-side revocation\n        self.session_store\n            .put(&token, user_id, self.session_ttl)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| AuthError::EventBus(e.into()))?;\n\n        Ok(Session {\n            token,\n            expires_at: now + self.session_ttl,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Validate a token and return the user id embedded in it.\n    pub async fn validate_token(&self, token: &str) -> Result<Uuid> {\n        // 1. Verify signature / expiration\n        let TokenData { claims, .. } = decode::<Claims>(\n            token,\n            &DecodingKey::from_secret(self.jwt_secret.as_bytes()),\n            &Validation::default(),\n        )?;\n\n        // 2. Check server-side revocation list (Redis)\n        if self\n            .session_store\n            .get_user_id(token)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| AuthError::EventBus(e.into()))?\n            .is_none()\n        {\n            return Err(AuthError::InvalidCredentials);\n        }\n\n        let uuid = Uuid::parse_str(&claims.sub).map_err(|_| AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n        Ok(uuid)\n    }\n}\n\n/// =============================================================================================\n/// REST handler glue (simplified for brevity). In a real project, this would live in actix-web or\n/// axum route modules. Here we show just enough to demonstrate SVC integration.\n/// =============================================================================================\n#[cfg(feature = \"rest_api\")]\npub mod rest {\n    use super::*;\n    use axum::{\n        extract::{Extension, Json},\n        http::StatusCode,\n        response::IntoResponse,\n        routing::post,\n        Router,\n    };\n    use std::sync::Arc;\n\n    #[derive(Clone)]\n    pub struct AppState<A> {\n        pub auth_service: Arc<A>,\n    }\n\n    pub fn router<A: 'static + Send + Sync>(\n        state: AppState<A>,\n    ) -> Router\n    where\n        A: AuthServiceTrait,\n    {\n        Router::new()\n            .route(\"/api/v1/auth/register\", post(register::<A>))\n            .route(\"/api/v1/auth/login\", post(login::<A>))\n            .layer(Extension(state))\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    pub trait AuthServiceTrait: Send + Sync {\n        async fn register(&self, new_user: NewUser) ->  115→\n   116→impl Dispatcher {\n   117→    /// Create a new dispatcher with a configurable ring buffer size.\n   118→    pub fn new(channel_size: usize) -> Self {\n   119→        let (sender, _rx) = An event–driven badge–awarding micro-service for the\n//! “EduPulse Live – Event-Driven Social Learning Hub” platform.\n//!\n//! This module demonstrates how the core application publishes\n//! domain events while an out-of-process, autonomous component\n//! (here: `BadgeAwardService`) consumes them, performs business\n//! logic, persists the result and publishes its own follow-up\n//! events.  \n//!\n//! The service is completely self-contained: it hosts an in-memory\n//! dispatcher for local development, yet persists events and state\n//! in Postgres so it can be moved into a distributed setting\n//! later on (e.g. with NATS/Kafka‐backed `Dispatcher`).\n//!\n//! Architectural patterns shown here:\n//!   • Repository Pattern      – decoupling persistence\n//!   • Service Layer           – `BadgeAwardService`\n//!   • Event Sourcing          – immutable `EventStore`\n//!   • Async Rust w/ Tokio     – non-blocking I/O\n//!   • Error Handling w/ thiserror + anyhow\n//!\n//! NOTE: In a real-world project this module would be split\n//! into several files/crates.  Everything is co-located here only\n//! to satisfy the single-file constraint of the exercise.\n\nuse std::{\n    collections::HashSet,\n    sync::Arc,\n    time::Duration,\n};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgPoolOptions, PgPool};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    select,\n   QuizSubmitted(QuizSubmitted),\n    BadgeAwarded(BadgeAwarded),\n    // … more variants omitted for brevity\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent {\n    /// Helper returning event name for logging / metrics.\n    pub fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted(_) => \"QuizSubmitted\",\n            DomainEvent::BadgeAwarded(_) => \"BadgeAwarded\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Metadata common to all envelopes—separates immutable event data\n/// from peripheral transport concerns such as IDs and timestamps.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub event: DomainEvent,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    pub fn new(event: DomainEvent) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n            event,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Event data triggered when a learner submits a quiz.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, might replace the implementation with NATS,\n/// Kafka, RabbitMQ… etc., leaving the public API intact.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct Dispatcher {\n    sender: broadcast::Sender<EventEnvelope>,\n}\n\nimpl Dispatcher {\n    /// Create a new dispatcher with a configurable ring buffer size.\n    pub fn new(channel_size: usize) -> Self {\n        let (sender, _rx) = broadcast::channel(channel_size);\n        Self { sender }\n    }\n\n    /// Publish an envelope to all subscribers.\n    pub async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) {\n        // broadcast::Sender::send is synchronous but inexpensive.\n        // Failure only occurs when there are no active receivers;\n        // still, we ignore that because the event is persisted in\n        // the store before reaching this point.\n        let _ = self.sender.send(envelope);\n    }\n\n    /// Subscribe to a continuous stream of events.\n    pub fn subscribe(&self) -> broadcast::Receiver<EventEnvelope> {\n        self.sender.subscribe()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Persisted event stream; provides idempotent append + read access.\n/// Uses Postgres JSONB for flexible schema evolution.\n///\n/// Table DDL (run via migrations):\n/// ```sql\n/// CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event_store (\n///     id           UUID PRIMARY KEY,\n///     occurred_at  TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE &str, max_connections: u32) -> Result<Self, sqlx::Error> {\n        let pool = PgPoolOptions::new()\n            .max_connections(max_connections)\n            .connect(db_url)\n            .await?;\n        Ok(Self { pool })\n    }\n\n    /// Append envelope to store.\n    pub async fn append(&self, env: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n read models.\n    pub async fn load_by_event(\n        &self,\n        event_name: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<EventEnvelope>, sqlx::Error> {\n        let recs = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, occurred_at, payload\n            FROM event_store\n            WHERE event_name = $1\n            ORDER BY occurred_at ASC\n            \"#,\n            event_name\n        )\n {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"Internal channel closed\")]\n    ChannelClosed,\n}\n\n/// Repository abstraction for badge persistence.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait            FROM learner_badges\n                WHERE learner_id = $1 AND badge_id = $2\n            ) AS \"exists!\"\n            \"#,\n            learner_id,\n            badge_id\n        )\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(res.unwrap_or(false))\n    }\n\n    async fn assign_badge(\n        &self,\n        learner_id: Uuid,\n        badge: &BadgeAwarded,\n    ) -> }\n}\n\n/// Business logic deciding whether a quiz submission qualifies\n/// for a badge. .into_iter()\n        .collect();\n\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            dispatcher,\n            event_store,\n            subscribed_badges,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn the service self.dispatcher.subscribe();\n\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            loop {\n                select! {\n                    biased;\n\n                    // React to new events\n                    msg = rx.recv() => match msg {\n                        Ok(env) => {\n                            if let Err(e) = self.process_event(env).await {\n Err(broadcast::error::RecvError::Lagged(skipped)) => {\n                            tracing::warn!(\"BadgeAwardService lagged, skipped {skipped} messages\");\n                        },\n   async fn process_event(&self, env: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        match env.event {\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted(sub) => self.maybe_award_badge(sub).await?,\n            _ => { /* ignore */ }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn maybe_award_badge(&self, sub: QuizSubmitted) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        // Determine qualifying badge.\n        let (badge_id, reason) = if (sub.score - 100.0).abs() < f32::EPSILON {\n            (\n                Uuid::parse_str(\"22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222\").unwrap(),\n                \"Perfect quiz score!\".to_owned(),\n            )\n        } else if sub.score >= 90.0 {\n            (\n                Uuid::parse_str(\"11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111\").unwrap(),\n                \"Scored 90% or above on a quiz.\".to_owned(),\n            )\n        } else {\n            return Ok(()); // Not eligible\n        };\n\n        // Filter unknown badge IDs (forward-compat).\n        if !self.subscribed_badges.contains(&badge_id) {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Idempotency check: learner may resubmit quiz multiple times.\n        if self\n            .repo\n            .learner_already_has_badge(sub.learner_id, badge_id)\n            .await?\n        {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Compose and persist badge award.\n    self.dispatcher.publish(envelope).await;\n\n        tracing::info!(\n            learner=%sub.learner_id,\n            badge=%badge.badge_id,\n            \"Badge awarded!\"\n        );\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n//  Bootstrap / Example\n// {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &(dyn DomainEvent + 'static)) -> Result<(), EventError> {\n        let mut cloned = Vec::new();\n        // Re-serialize to guarantee we send a **copy**   submission: &SubmissionCreated,\n    ) -> Result<PlagiarismResponse, EventError> {\n        #[derive(Serialize)]\n        struct Payload<'a> {\n            submission_id: &'a str,\n on a rubric repository.\n    async fn auto_score(&self, submission: &SubmissionCreated) -> Result<u8, EventError> {\n        let rubric = self.rubric_store.fetch_current().await;\n        let words = submission.content.split_whitespace().count() as u32;\n        let mut score = 0;\n\n        if words >= rubric.min_word_count {\n            score += 60;\n        }\n        if submission.content.contains(\"because\") {\n            score += 15;\n        }\n        if submission.content.contains(\"therefore\") {\n            score += 15;\n        }\n        if submission.content.ends_with('.') {\n            score += 10;\n        }\n        Ok(score.min(100) as u8)\n    }\n}\n\n/// DTO returned from the remote plagiarism service.\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]\nstruct PlagiarismApiPayload {\n    score: f32,\n    is_suspect: bool,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct PlagiarismResponse {\n    score: f32,\n    is_suspect: bool,\n}\n\nimpl }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simplistic rubric repository.\n/// In production this would talk to Postgres or Config Service.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct RubricRepository {\n    current: This module materialises the *Domain Event* concept mentioned in the\n     6→//! architecture description and provides a pluggable, async publisher that can\n     7→//! be wired to a real message-broker (e.g. RabbitMQ via *lapin*) or gracefully\n     8→//! degrade to a pure in-process logger in tests or local development.\n     9→//!\n    10→//! Key design points\n    11→//! -----------------\n    12→//! • A strongly-typed `DomainEvent` enum prevents the *stringly-typed* antipattern.  \n    13→//! • An `EventEnvelope` carries universal metadata used by downstream services.  \n    14→//! • The `EventPublisher` trait abstracts the transport; a concrete\n    15→//!   `RabbitMqPublisher` is behind a crate feature flag (`broker`).  \n    16→//! • `thiserror` provides ergonomic error handling.  \n    17→//! • `async_trait` allows `async` functions in traits while staying on stable Rust.\n    18→//!\n    19→//! -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    20→//! #[serde(skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    85→    pub correlation_id: Option<Uuid>,\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→impl Default for EventMetadata {\n    89→    fn default() -> Self {\n Event-driven infrastructure for **EduPulse Live – Event-Driven Social Learning\n//! Hub**.  This module materialises the *Domain Event* concept mentioned in the\n//! architecture description and provides a pluggable, async publisher that can\n//! be wired to a real message-broker (e.g. RabbitMQ via *lapin*) or gracefully\n//! degrade to a pure in-process logger in tests or local development.\n//!\n//! Key design points\n//! -----------------\n//! • A strongly-typed `DomainEvent` enum prevents the *stringly-typed* antipattern.  \n//! • An `EventEnvelope` carries universal metadata used by downstream services.  \n//! • The `EventPublisher` trait abstracts the transport; a concrete\n//!   `RabbitMqPublisher` is behind a crate feature flag (`broker`).  \n//! • `thiserror` provides ergonomic error handling.  \n//! • `async_trait` allows `async` functions in traits while staying on stable Rust.\n//!\n//! -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! Crate features\n//! --------------\n//! `broker` – Enables the real AMQP publisher that talks to RabbitMQ.  When the\n//! feature is **not** enabled, a lightweight logger implementation is compiled\n//! instead. This keeps test binaries small and fast.\n//!\n//! -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! External crates\n//! ---------------\n//!  - `serde`, `serde_json`  – (de)serialisation\n//!  - `uuid`                – event correlation / id\n//!  - `chrono`              – timestamps with timezone\n//!  - `async_trait`         – async in traits\n//!  - `thiserror`           – error enums\n//!  - `log`                 – structured logging facade\n//!  - `lapin`               – AMQP client  (only with `broker` feature)\n//!  - `tokio`               – async runtime, required by lapin\n//! -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#![allow(clippy::large_enum_variant)]\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse    publisher_confirm::Confirmation,\n        types::FieldTable,\n        BasicProperties, Channel, Connection, ConnectionProperties,\n    },\n    tokio::time::timeout,\n};\n\n/// Maximum time we wait to trace the\n    /// causal chain across service boundaries.\n    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    pub correlation_id: Option<Uuid>,\n}\n\nimpl Default for EventMetadata {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n            source: env!(\"CARGO_PKG_NAME\").to_owned(),\n            correlation_id: None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// The **DomainEvent** enum encapsulates the canonical vocabulary for the\n/// EduPulse domain.  Each variant is versioned so we can evolve payloads\n/// without breaking existing subscribers.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"payload\")]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    /// A teacher has published a new lesson.\n    LessonPublishedV1 {\n        lesson_id: Uuid,\n        title: String,\n        author_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A student submitted answers to a quiz.\n    QuizSubmittedV1 {\n        quiz_id: Uuid,\n        attempt_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A peer gave feedback to someone else's submission.\n    PeerFeedbackGivenV1 {\n        feedback_id: Uuid,\n        submission_id: Uuid,\n        reviewer_id: Uuid,\n    },\n\n    /// A student was granted a badge.\n    BadgeAwardedV1 {\n        badge_id: Uuid,\n        awardee_id: Uuid,\n        reason: String,\n    },\n\n    /// Money changed hands for a premium micro-course.\n    PaymentCapturedV1 {\n        payment_id: Uuid,\n        amount_cents: i64,\n        currency: String,\n        payer_id: Uuid,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Envelope that wraps the event payload with universal metadata.\n///\n/// Storing metadata *outside* the payload makes it trivial to evolve individual\n/// events without duplicating the same bookkeeping fields everywhere.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope {\n    pub meta: EventMetadata,\n    pub event: DomainEvent,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    pub fn new<E: Into<DomainEvent>>(event: E) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            meta: EventMetadata::default(),\n            event: event.into(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Serialise the envelope into a compact JSON payload.\n///\n/// We purposefully avoid pretty printing for network efficiency; Topic exchange\n    /// policies can fan-out events to multiple queues, e.g. `lesson.published.v1`.\n    pub fn routing_key(&self) -> String {\n        match &self.event {\n            DomainEvent::LessonPublishedV1 { .. } => \"lesson.published.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmittedV1 { .. } => \"quiz.submitted.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::PeerFeedbackGivenV1 { .. } => \"feedback.given.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::BadgeAwardedV1 { .. } => \"badge.awarded.v1\",\n            DomainEvent::PaymentCapturedV1 { .. } => \"payment.captured.v1\",\n        }\n        .into()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Error type for publishing and serialisation failures.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum EventError {\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serialization(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\n    #[error(\"amqp error: {0}\")]\n    Amqp(#[from] lapin::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"broker confirmation timed out\")]\n    ConfirmationTimeout,\n\n    #[error(\"other: {0}\")]\n    Other(String),\n}\n\n/// Trait every publisher (mock or real) must implement.\n///\n/// The abstraction enables dependency injection in application services and\n/// makes *unit* testing simpler:  we can just inject the `LoggingPublisher`.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError>;\n}\n\n/// A publisher that merely writes events to the `log` sink.\n///\n/// Enabled by default, also used when the `broker` feature is disabled. The\n/// design means the rest of the application can always depend on\n/// `dyn EventPublisher`, regardless of compilation mode.\npub struct LoggingPublisher;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for LoggingPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError> {\n        // Format into single-line JSON to keep log ingestion systems happy.\n        let json = envelope.to_json_bytes()?;\n        // Safe because JSON is UTF-8.\n        log::info!(\n            target: \"eduplse.events\",\n            \"event={} meta_id={} payload={}\",\n            envelope.routing_key(),\n            envelope.meta.id,\n            String::from_utf8_lossy(&json)\n        );\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Real AMQP publisher that talks to RabbitMQ.  \n///\n/// Compiled **only** when the `broker` feature is present.  Uses publisher\n/// confirms so we know the broker durably persisted the message.\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\npub struct RabbitMqPublisher {\n    channel: Channel,\n    exchange: String,\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\nimpl RabbitMqPublisher {\n    /// Connect to the AMQP broker and prepare the channel.\n    ///\n    /// A production-grade implementation would configure TLS, heartbeat, and\n    /// automatic reconnection. Those details are omitted for brevity.\n    pub async fn connect(\n        amqp_uri: &str,\n        exchange: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Self, EventError> {\n        let connection = Connection::connect(\n            amqp_uri,\n            ConnectionProperties::default().with_default_executor(8),\n        )\n        .await\n        .map_err(EventError::from)?;\n\n        let channel = connection.create_channel().await?;\n        channel\n            .confirm_select(lapin::options::ConfirmSelectOptions::default())\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            channel,\n            exchange: exchange.to_owned(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"broker\")]\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RabbitMqPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, envelope: EventEnvelope) -> Result<(), EventError> {\n        let payload = envelope.to_json_bytes()?;\n        let routing_key = envelope.routing_key();\n\n        let confirm: Confirmation = self\n            .channel\n            .basic_publish(\n                &self.exchange,\n                &routing_key,\n                BasicPublishOptions {\n                    mandatory: false,\n                    immediate: false,\n                },\n                &payload,\n                BasicProperties::default().with_content_type(\"application/json\"),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await?; // Wait for broker confirm (publisher confirms must be enabled)\n\n        // lapin returns Nack(…) in error path; we check for `Ack`.\n        if !confirm.is_ack() {\n            return Err(EventError::Other(format!(\n                \"broker did not acknowledge event id {}\",\n                envelope.meta.id\n            )));\n        }\n\n        // Additionally enforce a timeout because `await?` can hang if broker is\n        // misconfigured (e.g., confirm_select was forgotten).\n        timeout(PUBLISH_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT, async {\n            while !confirm.is_ack() {}\n        })\n        .await\n        .map_err(|_| EventError::ConfirmationTimeout)?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// When compiled **without** the `broker` feature, alias Result<LearningPulse, AppError> {\n        // --- Validation ----------------------------------------------------\n        if title.trim().is_empty() {\n            return Err(AppError::Validation(\"title may not be empty\".into()));\n        }\n        if body.trim().is_empty() {\n            return Err(AppError::Validation(\"body may not be empty\".into()));\n        }\n\n        // --- Entity construction -------------------------------------------\n        let now = Utc::now();\n        let pulse = LearningPulse {\n            id: uuid::Uuid::new_v4(),\n            teacher_id,\n            title,\n            body,\n            tags,\n            created_at: now,\n            updated_at: now,\n        };\n\n        // --- Persistence ----------------------------------------------------\n        self.repo.insert(pulse.clone()).await?;\n\n        // --- Event emission --------------------------------------------------\n        let ev = DomainEvent::LearningPulseCreated {\n            pulse_id: pulse.id,\n            teacher_id: pulse.teacher_id,\n            timestamp: now,\n        };\n        // Send to buffer; do not await publisher here.\n        if let Err(e) = self.tx.send(ev).await {\n            tracing::error!(error = ?e, \"event buffer channel closed unexpectedly\");\n        }\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n\n .expect(\"DATABASE_URL must be set\");\n\n    let manager = ConnectionManager::<PgConnection>::new(database_url);\n    let pool = r2d2::Pool::builder()\n        .max_size(20)\n        .connection_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))\n        .build(manager)?;\n\n    let repo = Arc::new(DieselLearningPulseRepo::new(pool));\n    let publisher = 168→\n   169→    /// Append a *single* event to the store with OCC.\n   170→    ///\n   171→    /// Returns the stored [`EventEnvelope`] (with auto-generated id + timestamp).\n   172→    #[instrument(skip(self, tx))]\n   173→    pub async fn append_one(\n   174→        &self,\n   175→        mut tx: Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n   176→        aggregate_id: AggregateId,\n   177→        expected_seq: i64,\n   178→        event: DomainEvent,\n   179→        correlation_id: Option<EventId>,\n   180→    ) -> Result<EventEnvelope> {\n   181→        // Next sequence number we intend to store.\n   182→        let next_seq = expected_seq + 1;\n   183→\n   184→        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(aggregate_id, next_seq, event, correlation_id);\n   185→        let serialized = serde_json::to_value(&envelope)?;\n   186→\n   187→        let res = sqlx::query(\n   188→            r#\"\n   189→            INSERT INTO event_store (id, aggregate_id, seq, payload)\n   190→            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n   191→            ON CONFLICT (aggregate_id, seq) DO NOTHING\n   192→            \"#,\n   193→        )\n   194→        .bind(envelope.id.0)\n   195→        .bind(envelope.aggregate_id)\n   196→        .bind(envelope.seq)\n   197→        .bind(&serialized)\n   198→        .execute(&mut *tx)\n   199→        .await?;\n   200→\n   201→        if res.rows_affected() == 0 {\n   202→            // Sequence already taken – someone else wrote concurrently.\n   203→            let actual = self\n   204→                .latest_sequence_for(&aggregate_id)\n   205→                .await?\n   206→                .unwrap_or_default();\n   207→\n   208→            return Err(EventError::Concurrency {\n   209→                aggregate_id,\n   210→                expected: expected_seq,\n   211→                actual,\n   212→            });\n   213→        }\n   214→\n   215→        Ok(envelope)\n   216→    }\n   217→\n   218→    /// Returns the *last* known sequence for an aggregate.  If absent, returns\n   219→    /// `None`.\n   220→    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n   221→    pub async fn latest_sequence_for(&self, aggregate_id: &AggregateId) -> Result<Option<i64>> {\n   222→        let seq = sqlx::query_scalar::<_, Option<i64>>(\n   223→            r#\"\n   224→            SELECT seq\n   225→            FROM event_store\n   226→            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n   227→            ORDER BY seq DESC\n   228→            LIMIT 1\n   229→            \"#,\n   230→        )\n   231→        .bind(aggregate_id)\n   232→        .fetch_one(&*self.pool)\n   233→        .await?;\n   234→\n   235→        Ok(seq)\n   236→    }\n   237→\n   238→    /// Open an *unbounded* stream for the whole event log.\n   239→    /// Clients should apply their own back-pressure strategies.\n   240→    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n   241→    pub fn stream_all(&self) -> impl Stream<Item = Result<EventEnvelope>> + '_ {\n   242→        sqlx::query_scalar::<_, serde_json::Value>(\n   243→            r#\"SELECT payload FROM event_store ORDER BY id\"#,\n   244→        )\n   245→        .fetch(&*self.pool)\n   246→        .map_ok(|val| serde_json::from_value::<EventEnvelope>(val))\n   247→        .try_buffered(64) // concurrent JSON deserialization\n   248→    }\n   249→\n   250→    /// Helper to begin a transaction.\n   251→    pub async fn begin(&self) -> Result<Transaction<'_, Postgres>> op))]\n   325→    pub async fn commit<F, Fut, T>(&self, op: F) -> Result<T>\n   326→    where\n   327→        F: FnOnce(&Self, &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>) -> Fut + Send + Sync,\n   328→        Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T>> + Send,\n   329→        T: Send,\n   330→    {\n   331→        let mut tx = self.store.begin().await?;\n   332→\n   333→        let result = op(self, &mut 353→        // Publish *after* commit to avoid phantom events.\n   354→        for env in staged_events {\n   355→            // We could `spawn` but prefer sequential to simplify ordering/acks.\n   356→            self.publisher.publish(&env).await?;\n   357→        }\n   358→\n   359→        Ok(result)\n   360→    }\n   361→\n   362→    /// Convenience wrapper: append + publish inside a running 390→        .execute(&mut *tx)\n   391→        .await?;\n   392→\n   393→        Ok(env)\n   394→    }\n   395→}\n   396→\n   397→// pub async fn append_one(\n        &self,\n        mut tx: Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        aggregate_id: AggregateId,\n        expected_seq: i64,\n        event: DomainEvent,\n        correlation_id: Option<EventId>,\n    ) -> Result<EventEnvelope> {\n        // Next sequence number we intend to store.\n        let next_seq = expected_seq + 1;\n\n        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(aggregate_id, next_seq, event, correlation_id);\n        let serialized = serde_json::to_value(&envelope)?;\n\n        let res = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO event_store (id, aggregate_id, seq, payload)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)\n            ON CONFLICT (aggregate_id, seq) DO NOTHING\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(envelope.id.0)\n        .bind(envelope.aggregate_id)\n        .bind(envelope.seq)\n        .bind(&serialized)\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        if res.rows_affected() == 0 {\n            // Sequence already taken – someone else wrote concurrently.\n            let actual = self\n                .latest_sequence_for(&aggregate_id)\n                .await?\n                .unwrap_or_default();\n\n            return Err(EventError::Concurrency {\n                aggregate_id,\n                expected: expected_seq,\n                actual,\n            });\n        }\n\n        Ok(envelope)\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the *last* known sequence for an aggregate.  If absent, returns\n    /// `None`.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn latest_sequence_for(&self, aggregate_id: &AggregateId) -> Result<Option<i64>> {\n        let seq = sqlx::query_scalar::<_, Option<i64>>(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT seq\n            FROM event_store\n            WHERE aggregate_id = $1\n            ORDER BY seq DESC\n            LIMIT 1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(aggregate_id)\n        .fetch_one(&*self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(seq)\n    }\n\n    /// Open an *unbounded* stream for the whole event log.\n    /// Clients should apply their own back-pressure strategies.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub fn stream_all(&self) -> impl Stream<Item = Result<EventEnvelope>> + '_ {\n        sqlx::query_scalar::<_, serde_json::Value>(\n            r#\"SELECT payload FROM event_store ORDER BY id\"#,\n        )\n        .fetch(&*self.pool)\n        .map_ok(|val| serde_json::from_value::<EventEnvelope>(val))\n        .try_buffered(64) // concurrent JSON deserialization\n    }\n\n    /// Helper to begin a transaction.\n    pub async fn begin(&self) -> Result<Transaction<'_, Postgres>> {\n        Ok(self.pool.begin().await?)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Publishes events to NATS with at-least-once semantics.\n///\n/// It is intentionally *thin*: we keep transport concerns outside domain\n/// services.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct EventPublisher {\n    nats: NatsClient,\n    subject_prefix: Arc<str>,\n}\n\nimpl EventPublisher {\n    pub async fn connect(nats_url: &str, subject_prefix: impl AsRef<str>) -> Result<Self> {\n        // TLS enabled by default (async_nats w/ `tls` feature).\n        let nats = async_nats::connect(nats_url).await?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            nats,\n            subject_prefix: Arc::from(subject_prefix.as_ref()),\n        })\n    }\n\n    fn subject_for(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> String {\n        // Use enum variant name as the subject suffix.\n        let variant = match event {\n            DomainEvent::LessonPublished { .. } => \"LessonPublished\",\n            DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted { .. } => \"QuizSubmitted\",\n            DomainEvent::BadgeAwarded { .. } => \"BadgeAwarded\",\n            DomainEvent::PaymentProcessed { .. } => \"PaymentProcessed\",\n        };\n        format!(\"{}.{}\", self.subject_prefix, variant)\n    }\n\n    /// Publish an envelope to NATS.  Returns the `PublishAck`.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, envelope))]\n    pub async fn publish(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<PublishAck> {\n        let subject = self.subject_for(&envelope.event);\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(envelope)?;\n\n        Ok(self.nats.publish(subject, payload.into()).await?)\n    }\n}\n\n/// High-level facade combining the store and broker for transactional\n/// *outbox*-style consistency.\n///\n/// Usage:\n///\n/// ```rust,no_run\n/// let ctx = EventContext::new(store, publisher);\n/// ctx.commit(|tx| async move {\n///     // ... domain logic mutating aggregates ...\n///     ctx.append(tx, aggregate_id, expected_seq, event).await?;\n///     Ok(())\n/// }).await?;\n/// ```\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct EventContext {\n    store: EventStore,\n    publisher: EventPublisher,\n}\n\nimpl EventContext {\n    pub fn new(store: EventStore, publisher: EventPublisher) -> Self {\n        Self { store, publisher }\n    }\n\n    /// Wrap a *business* operation in a transaction and publish all newly\n    /// appended events atomically (two-phase commit).\n    #[instrument(skip(self, op))]\n    pub async fn commit<F, Fut, T>(&self, op: F) -> Result<T>\n    where\n        F: FnOnce(&Self, &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>) -> Fut + Send + Sync,\n        Fut: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T>> + Send,\n        T: Send,\n    {\n        let mut tx = self.store.begin().await?;\n\n        let result = op(self, &mut tx).await?;\n\n        // Extract fresh events written within the tx:\n        let staged_events: Vec<EventEnvelope> = sqlx::query_scalar(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT payload\n            FROM event_store\n            WHERE transaction_timestamp() - statement_timestamp() < INTERVAL '1 second'\n              AND txid_current() = txid_current()\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&mut *tx)\n        .await?\n        .into_iter()\n        .map(|val: serde_json::Value| serde_json::from_value(val))\n        .collect::<std::result::Result<_, _>>()?;\n\n        // Commit DB first (outbox pattern).\n        tx.commit().await?;\n\n        // Publish *after* commit to avoid phantom events.\n        for env in staged_events {\n            // We could `spawn` but prefer sequential to simplify ordering/acks.\n            self.publisher.publish(&env).await?;\n        }\n\n        Ok(result)\n    }\n\n    /// Convenience wrapper: append + publish inside a running tx.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, tx))]\n    pub async fn append(\n        &self,\n        tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n        aggregate_id: AggregateId,\n        expected_seq: i64,\n        event: DomainEvent,\n        correlation_id: Option<EventId>,\n    ) -> Result<EventEnvelope> {\n        let env = self\n            .store\n            .append_one(\n                tx.as_mut(),\n                aggregate_id,\n                expected_seq,\n                event,\n                correlation_id,\n            )\n            .await?;\n\n        // Buffer the event in the transaction’s local temp table for later pickup.\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO _staged_events (payload) VALUES ($1)\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(sqlx::types::Json(&env))\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(env)\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Database bootstrap helpers (run via migrations)\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Creates the *event_store* table and helper structures.\n///\n/// Called by the migration runner (e.g., refinery, sqlx-migrate).\npub async fn bootstrap_event_schema(pool: &PgPool) -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n    let mut conn = pool.acquire().await?;\n\n    // Use idempotent `IF NOT EXISTS` to keep it re-runnable.\n    sqlx::query(\n        r#\"\n        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event_store (\n            id            UUID            PRIMARY KEY,\n            aggregate_id  UUID            NOT NULL,\n            seq           BIGINT          NOT NULL,\n            payload       JSONB           NOT NULL,\n\n            CONSTRAINT seq_unique UNIQUE (aggregate_id, seq)\n        );\n\n        -- Fast lookup for aggregates\n        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_event_store_aggregate\n            ON event_store (aggregate_id);\n\n        -- Staging area for outbox pattern (tx-scoped)\n        CREATE TEMP TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _staged_events (\n            payload JSONB NOT NULL\n        ) ON COMMIT DROP;\n        \"#,\n    )\n    .execute(&mut conn)\n    .await?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n//      bootstrap_event_schema(&store.pool).await?;\n\n        let publisher = EventPublisher::connect(&nats_url, \"edupulse.events\").await?;\n        let ctx = EventContext::new(store.clone(), workers\n//! event_bus.register_handler(SearchIndexer::new()?); // Tantivy search handler\n//! event_bus.emit(DomainEvent::LessonPublished {...}).await?;\n//! ```\n\nuse std::{\n    sync::Arc,\n    time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH},\n};\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse dashmap::DashMap;\nuse serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse tokio::{\n    select,\n    spawn,\n    sync::{broadcast, mpsc},\n    task::JoinHandle,\n};\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument, warn};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Default broadcast channel capacity (per Tokio docs, power of two for perf)\nconst CHANNEL_CAPACITY: usize = 1 << 8;\n\n/// All domain-level events that flow through the platform.\n///\n/// When this enum grows large, consider splitting variants into separate enums\n/// and boxing them in an `enum`/`trait object` for compile-time boundary\n/// management.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"data\")]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    LessonPublished {\n        lesson_id: Uuid,\n        author_id: Uuid,\n        title: String,\n        published_at: UnixTimestamp,\n    },\n    QuizSubmitted {\n        quiz_id: Uuid,\n        student_id: Uuid,\n        score: u8,\n        submitted_at: UnixTimestamp,\n    },\n    // --- truncated: add more events as needed ---\n}\n\n/// Simple millisecond-precision unix timestamp wrapper.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct UnixTimestamp(u64);\n\nimpl Default for UnixTimestamp {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self(\n            SystemTime::now()\n                .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n                .expect(\"time went backwards\")\n                .as_millis() as u64,\n        )\n    }\n}\n\n/// Persistence façade for storing domain events.\n///\n/// The event store is append-only and can be replayed for audits, projections\n/// or rebuilding read models.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventStore: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn append(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<()>;\n    async fn stream_all<E>(&self) -> Result<Vec<E>>\n    where\n        E: DeserializeOwned + Send + 'static;\n}\n\n/// In-memory reference implementation; suitable for tests / dev playground.\n#[derive(Debug, Default)]\npub struct InMemoryEventStore {\n    events: tokio::sync::RwLock<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventStore for InMemoryEventStore {\n    #[instrument(skip(self, event))]\n    async fn append(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<()> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event).context(\"serialising event\")?;\n        self.events.write().await.push(payload);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn stream_all<E>(&self) -> Result<Vec<E>>\n    where\n        E: DeserializeOwned + Send + 'static,\n    {\n        let raw = self.events.read().await.clone();\n        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(raw.len());\n        for bytes in raw {\n            let evt = serde_json::from_slice::<E>(&bytes).context(\"deserialising\")?;\n            out.push(evt);\n        }\n        Ok(out)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Event handler contract.\n///\n/// Note: we purposefully consume by value (`self`) to allow stateful handlers to\n/// move into the async task when registered.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventHandler: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    /// A human-readable name for observability.\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n\n    /// Business logic invoked for each event.\n    async fn handle(&self, evt: DomainEvent) -> Result<()>;\n}\n\n/// Thread-safe, async event bus.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct EventBus<S: EventStore> {\n    tx: broadcast::Sender<DomainEvent>,\n    store: Arc<S>,\n    handlers: DashMap<&'static str, Box<dyn EventHandler>>,\n    /// Workers receive events from the broadcast channel and sequentially\n    /// deliver them to handlers (de-duplicated per handler).\n    worker_txs: Vec<mpsc::Sender<DomainEvent>>,\n}\n\nimpl<S: EventStore> EventBus<S> {\n    pub fn new(store: S) -> Self {\n        let (tx, _rx) = broadcast::channel(CHANNEL_CAPACITY);\n        Self {\n            tx,\n            store: Arc::new(store),\n            handlers: DashMap::new(),\n            worker_txs: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Emit a single event.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn emit(&self, evt: DomainEvent) -> Result<()> {\n        self.store.append(&evt).await?;\n        self.tx\n            .send(evt)\n            .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!(\"broadcast failure: {e}\"))?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Register a handler; idempotent per handler name.\n    pub fn register_handler<H>(&self, handler: H)\n    where\n        H: EventHandler,\n    {\n        if self.handlers.insert(handler.name(), Box::new(handler)).is_some() {\n            warn!(\"handler {} replaced at runtime\", handler.name());\n        } else {\n            info!(\"handler {} registered\", handler.name());\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn a worker pool that fans-out events to handlers.\n    ///\n    /// Each worker owns an mpsc channel (to avoid contention) and reads from\n    /// the *same* broadcast channel.  This guarantees at-least-once delivery to\n    /// each handler.\n    pub fn start_worker_pool(self: &Arc<Self>, workers: usize) -> Vec<JoinHandle<()>> {\n        let mut handles = Vec::with_capacity(workers);\n        for id in 0..workers {\n            let (tx, mut rx) = mpsc::channel::<DomainEvent>(CHANNEL_CAPACITY);\n            self.worker_txs.push(tx);\n\n            let bus = Arc::clone(self);\n            let mut broadcast_rx = bus.tx.subscribe();\n            handles.push(spawn(async move {\n                info!(worker = id, \"event worker started\");\n                loop {\n                    select! {\n                        Ok(evt) = broadcast_rx.recv() => {\n                            // fan-in to single queue\n                            if let Err(e) = rx.try_send(evt) {\n                                debug!(%e, worker = id, \"worker queue full; dropping\");\n                            }\n                        },\n\n                        Some(evt) = rx.recv() => {\n                            for item in bus.handlers.iter() {\n                                let handler = item.value();\n                                let evt_clone = evt.clone();\n                                let h_name = handler.name();\n                                let res = handler.handle(evt_clone).await;\n                                if let Err(e) = res {\n                                    error!(%e, handler = h_name, \"event handler failed\");\n                                }\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }));\n        }\n        handles\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/* ----------------------- Example Production Handler ----------------------- */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Tantivy-powered search indexer.\n///\n/// In a real deployment this would:\n/// 1. Open a persistent `Index` directory;\n/// 2. Map domain events to documents;\n/// 3. Commit in batches for throughput.\npub struct SearchIndexer {\n    // index: tantivy::Index,\n}\n\nimpl SearchIndexer {\n    pub fn new() -> Result<Self> {\n        // let index = tantivy::Index::open_or_create(...)?;\n        Ok(Self {\n            // index,\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventHandler for SearchIndexer {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"search_indexer\"\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    async fn handle(&self, evt: DomainEvent) -> Result<()> {\n        match evt {\n            DomainEvent::LessonPublished {\n                lesson_id,\n                title,\n                author_id,\n                ..\n            } => {\n                debug!(\n                    ?lesson_id,\n                    %title,\n                    ?author_id,\n                    \"indexing lesson into search engine\"\n                );\n                // let mut writer = self.index.writer(50_000_000)?;\n                // writer.add_document(...);\n                // writer.commit()?;\n            }\n            _ => { /* ignore other events */ }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* \"multi_thread\", worker_threads = 2)]\n    async fn event_roundtrip() -> Result<()> {\n        tracing_subscriber::fmt::try_init().ok();\n\n        let bus = Arc::new(EventBus::new(InMemoryEventStore::default()));\n        bus.register_handler(SearchIndexer::new()?);\n        bus.start_worker_pool(2);\n\n        let evt = DomainEvent::LessonPublished {\n Deserialize)]\npub struct EventEnvelope<E: DomainEvent> {\n    pub event_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub event_type: &'static str,\n    pub version: &'static str,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub payload: E,\n}\n\n/// Helper to wrap a concrete event instance.\npub fn wrap_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n    aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    payload: E,\n) -> EventEnvelope<E> {\n    EventEnvelope {\n        event_id: uuid::Uuid::new_v4(),\n        event_type: payload.event_type(),\n        version: payload.version(),\n        occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        aggregate_id,\n        payload,\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Outbox persistence\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Row inside `event_outbox` table.\n///\n/// Inserted within the same DB transaction as the originating command.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, sqlx::FromRow)]\npub struct OutboxEvent {\n    pub id: i64,\n    pub aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub event_type: String,\n    pub version: String,\n    pub payload: serde_json::Value,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub published_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/// Persist a new domain event in the outbox table.\n///\n/// Must be invoked within the caller's SQL transaction.\n#[instrument(skip(tx, envelope))]\npub async fn persist_event<E>(\n    tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n    envelope: &EventEnvelope<E>,\n) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\nwhere\n    E: DomainEvent,\n{\n    let payload_json = serde_json::to_value(&envelope.payload)?;\n    sqlx::query!(\n        r#\"\n        INSERT INTO event_outbox \n            (aggregate_id, event_type, version, payload, occurred_at)\n        VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)\n        \"#,\n        envelope.aggregate_id,\n        envelope.event_type,\n        envelope.version,\n        payload_json,\n        envelope.occurred_at,\n    )\n    .execute(&mut *tx)\n    .await?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Outbox processor implementation\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Number of events we fetch per batch to reduce DB contention.\nconst BATCH_SIZE: i64 = 250;\n\n/// Processor error surface.\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum OutboxError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Db(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"NATS error: {0}\")]\n    Nats(#[from] async_nats::Error),\n}\n\n/// Background worker which iteratively drains the `event_outbox` table\n/// and forwards the events to NATS JetStream.\npub struct OutboxProcessor {\n    pg_pool: PgPool,\n    nats: jetstream::Context,\n}\n\nimpl OutboxProcessor {\n    /// Constructs a new [`OutboxProcessor`].\n    pub async fn new(pg_pool: PgPool, nats_url: &str) -> Result<Self, OutboxError> {\n        let nats_client = NatsClient::connect_with_options(\n            nats_url,\n            ConnectOptions::with_name(\"edupulse-outbox\"),\n        )\n        .await?;\n        let jetstream = jetstream::new(nats_client);\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            pg_pool,\n            nats: jetstream,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Starts the infinite polling loop until `shutdown` future resolves.\n    ///\n    /// Caller decides the sleep interval between attempts.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, shutdown), name = \"outbox.run\")]\n    pub async fn run_until_stopped(\n        &self,\n        poll_interval: Duration,\n        mut shutdown: tokio::sync::watch::Receiver<()>,\n    ) {\n        loop {\n            tokio::select! {\n                _ = shutdown.changed() => {\n                    info!(\"Outbox processor received shutdown signal – finishing current batch\");\n                    break;\n                }\n                _ = self.tick() => {}\n            }\n\n            sleep(poll_interval).await;\n        }\n\n        info!(\"Outbox processor terminated gracefully\");\n    }\n\n    /// Execute a single polling cycle.\n    #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn tick(&self) {\n        match self.process_batch().await {\n            Ok(count) => {\n                if count > 0 {\n                    info!(count, \"outbox events dispatched\");\n                } else {\n                    debug!(\"no events to dispatch\");\n                }\n            }\n            Err(err) => {\n                error!(?err, \"failed to process outbox batch\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Fetch a batch of pending events, publish, and mark as dispatched.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), err)]\n    async fn process_batch(&self) -> Result<u64, OutboxError> {\n        let mut tx = self.pg_pool.begin().await?;\n\n        // Step 1: select for update – prevents concurrent processors\n        let events: Vec<OutboxEvent> = sqlx::query_as!(\n            OutboxEvent,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT * FROM event_outbox\n            WHERE published_at IS NULL\n            ORDER BY id\n            FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED\n            LIMIT $1\n            \"#,\n            BATCH_SIZE\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        if events.is_empty() {\n            tx.rollback().await?;\n            return Ok(0);\n        }\n\n        // Step 2: publish each event to NATS\n        for evt in &events {\n            let payload_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&evt)?;\n            self.nats\n                .publish(NATS_SUBJECT, payload_bytes.into())\n                .await?;\n        }\n\n        // Step 3: update published stamp\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            UPDATE event_outbox\n            SET published_at = NOW()\n            WHERE id = ANY($1)\n            \"#,\n            &events.iter().map(|e| e.id).collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        )\n        .execute(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        Ok(events.len() as u64)\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Example domain event\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Fired when a new learning pulse (challenge) is created by a teacher.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LearningPulseCreated {\n    pub pulse_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub author_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub summary: String,\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent for LearningPulseCreated {\n    fn event_type(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"learning_pulse.created\"\n    }\n\n    fn version(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"1.0\"\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Convenience API for application services\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Publish a newly generated domain event inside an existing SQL transaction.\n///\n/// Typical usage in a service command handler:\n///\n/// ```ignore\n/// let mut tx = pool.begin().await?;\n/// let envelope = wrap_event(aggregate_id, LearningPulseCreated { … });\n/// persist_event(&mut tx, &envelope).await?;\n/// tx.commit().await?;\n/// ```\npub async fn publish_domain_event<E>(\n    tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n    aggregate_id: uuid::Uuid,\n    event: E,\n) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\nwhere\n    E: DomainEvent,\n{\n    let envelope = wrap_event(aggregate_id, event);\n    persist_event(tx, &envelope).await\n}\n```","numLines":311,"startLine":1,"totalLines":311}}}

    .map_err(|_| EventBusError::Timeout)?\n   229→        .map_err(EventBusError::MessageBroker)?;\n   230→\n   231→        info!(subject, \"Event published to NATS\");\n   232→        Ok(())\n   233→    }\n   234→\n   235→    /// Replays events of a given aggregate for state reconstruction (CQRS).\n   236→    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n   237→    pub async fn stream_for<T>(&self, aggregate_id: Uuid) .fetch_all(&self.db)\n   252→        .await\n   253→        .map_err(EventBusError::Database)?;\n   254→\n   255→        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(records.len());\n   256→        for (id, }\n   266→        Ok(out)\n   267→    }\n   268→}\n   269→\n   270→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   271→/// ERRORS\n   272→/// Event-sourcing infrastructure for EduPulse Live.\n//\n//  This module is responsible for:\n//\n//  1.  Defining domain-event primitives used across bounded contexts.\n//  2.  Persisting events to Postgres (append-only event store).\n//  3.  Publishing events to NATS for inter-service communication.\n//\n//  The implementation follows the Repository + Service Layer pattern.\n//  All operations are asynchronous and cancellation-safe.\n//\n//  Production quality considerations:\n//\n//  •   Events are idempotent (id is always generated server-side).\n//  •   Structured logging via `tracing`.\n//  •   Resilient error handling with `thiserror`.\n//  •   Secure JSON serialization (deny unknown fields).\n//  •   Observability hooks (OpenTelemetry compatible).\n//\n//  To keep the example self-contained, migrations and consumer side are\n//  omitted. In a real deployment, refer to `migrations/20240229101000_create_event_store.sql`.\n\n#![allow(clippy::missing_docs_in_private_items)]\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_nats::{Client as NatsClient, ConnectionOptions};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgPoolOptions, PgPool, Postgres, QueryBuilder};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::time::timeout;\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument, warn};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Maximum time allotted for a single database or network operation.\n///\n/// NOTE: Tokio’s cooperative scheduling may add a small overhead.\nconst IO_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(3);\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// DOMAIN-EVENT INFRASTRUCTURE\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n///   Every domain event MUST implement this marker trait.\n///\n///   `Serialize` ensures we can persist / publish the event payload.\n///   `Send + Sync + 'static` are required to cross async ‑await boundaries safely.\npub trait DomainEvent: Serialize + Send + Sync + 'static {\n    /// Fully-qualified name used by subscribers for routing.\n    ///\n    /// String instead of `TypeId` to keep binary compatibility across FFI boundaries\n    /// (e.g. WASM plugins).\n    fn kind() -> &'static str;\n}\n\n/// Envelope persisted to the event store and emitted on the message bus.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"camelCase\", deny_unknown_fields)]\npub struct EventEnvelope<T: DomainEvent> {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub payload: T,\n}\n\nimpl<T: DomainEvent> EventEnvelope<T> {\n    pub fn new(aggregate_id: Uuid, payload: T) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            aggregate_id,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n DATABASE_URL, NATS_URL, NATS_TOKEN (optional).\n    pub async fn from_env() -> Result<Self, EventBusError> {\n        let database_url = std::env::var(\"DATABASE_URL\")\n            .map_err(|_| EventBusError::MissingEnvVar(\"DATABASE_URL\".into()))?;\n        let nats_url =\n            std::env::var(\"NATS_URL\").unwrap_or_else(|_| \"nats://127.0.0.1:4222\".to_string());\n\n        let db = PgPoolOptions::new()\n            .max_connections(10)\n            .acquire_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5))\n            .connect(&database_url)\n            .await?;\n\n        // Build NATS connection options (timeouts, compression, TLS, etc.).\n        let mut nats_opts = ConnectionOptions::new();\n        nats_opts = nats_opts.name(\"edupulse_live_event_bus\");\n        if let Ok(token) = std::env::var(\"NATS_TOKEN\") {\n            nats_opts = nats_opts.auth_token(token);\n        }\n\n        let nats = nats_opts.connect(&nats_url).await?;\n\n        Ok(Self { db, nats })\n    }\n\n    ///   Persist + publish a single domain event.\n    ///\n    ///   Atomicity guarantees:\n    ///   1.  We always persist *before* publishing.\n    ///   2.  In case of a process crash after COMMIT but before PUB,\n    ///       the outbox pattern with a background re-publisher SHALL be used.\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(event = %T::kind()))]\n    pub async fn dispatch<T>(&self, aggregate_id: Uuid, event: T) -> Result<(), EventBusError>\n    where\n        T: DomainEvent,\n    {\n        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(aggregate_id, event);\n        // 1. Persist\n        self.append_to_store(&envelope).await?;\n\n        // 2. Publish\n        self.publish_to_bus(&envelope).await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Append event to the Postgres event store.\n    async fn append_to_store<T>(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope<T>) -> Result<(), EventBusError>\n    where\n        T: DomainEvent,\n    {\n        // Serialize payload as compact JSON.\n        let payload_json =\n            serde_json::to_value(&envelope.payload).map_err(EventBusError::Serialization)?;\n\n        let mut qb: QueryBuilder<Postgres> =\n            QueryBuilder::new(\"INSERT INTO event_store(id, aggregate_id, kind, occurred_at, payload) \");\n        qb.push_values(\n            std::iter::once(()),\n            |mut b, _| {\n                b.push_bind(envelope.id)\n                    .push_bind(envelope.aggregate_id)\n                    .push_bind(T::kind())\n                    .push_bind(envelope.occurred_at)\n                    .push_bind(payload_json);\n            },\n        );\n\n        let query = qb.build();\n        timeout(IO_TIMEOUT, query.execute(&self.db))\n            .await\n            .map_err(|_| EventBusError::Timeout)?\n            .map_err(EventBusError::Database)?;\n\n        debug!(\n            aggregate_id = %envelope.aggregate_id,\n            event_id = %envelope.id,\n            \"Event persisted\"\n        );\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Publish event to NATS.\n    async fn publish_to_bus<T>(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope<T>) -> Result<(), EventBusError>\n    where\n        T: DomainEvent,\n    {\n        // Serialize *entire* envelope to JSON so downstream services receive metadata.\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(envelope).map_err(EventBusError::Serialization)?;\n\n        let subject = T::kind();\n\n        timeout(\n            IO_TIMEOUT,\n            self.nats\n                .publish(subject.into(), payload.into())\n                .await\n                .map(|_| ()),\n        )\n        .await\n        .map_err(|_| EventBusError::Timeout)?\n        .map_err(EventBusError::MessageBroker)?;\n\n        info!(subject, \"Event published to NATS\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Replays events of a given aggregate for state reconstruction (CQRS).\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    pub async fn stream_for<T>(&self, aggregate_id: Uuid) -> Result<Vec<EventEnvelope<T>>, EventBusError>\n    where\n        T: DomainEvent + DeserializeOwned,\n    {\n        let records: Vec<(Uuid, DateTime<Utc>, serde_json::Value)> = sqlx::query_as(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, occurred_at, payload\n            FROM   event_store\n            WHERE  aggregate_id = $1 AND kind = $2\n            ORDER  BY occurred_at ASC\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(aggregate_id)\n        .bind(T::kind())\n        .fetch_all(&self.db)\n        .await\n        .map_err(EventBusError::Database)?;\n\n        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(records.len());\n        for (id, occurred_at, payload) in records {\n            let payload: T =\n                serde_json::from_value(payload).map_err(EventBusError::Deserialization)?;\n            out.push(EventEnvelope {\n                id,\n                aggregate_id,\n                occurred_at,\n                payload,\n            });\n        }\n        Ok(out)\n    }\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// ERRORS\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum EventBusError {\n    #[error(\"missing env var: {0}\")]\n    MissingEnvVar(String),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serialization(serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"deserialization error: {0}\")]\n    Deserialization(serde_json::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Database(sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"message-broker error: {0}\")]\n    MessageBroker(async_nats::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"operation timed out\")]\n    Timeout,\n}\n\n/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// UNIT TESTS\n/// This module implements the Outbox pattern and provides\n//! secure, reliable publishing of domain events to the system\n//! message broker (NATS in the reference deployment).\n//!\n//! .map(|evt| {\n                    let conn = &nats_conn;\n                    async move {\n                        conn.publish(evt.subject(), serde_json::to_vec(&evt)?)\n                            .await\n                            .map_err(OutboxError::from)\n                    }\n                })\n                .buffer_unordered(16)\n                .collect::<Vec<_>>();\n\n Vec<_> = events\n                    .iter()\n                    .zip(oks)\n                    .filter_map(|(evt, res)| res.ok().map(|_| evt.clone()))\n                    .collect();\n\n tracing::warn!(\n                    failed = errs.len(),\n                    \"Some messages failed to publish; will retry\"\n                );\n                // Exponential-ish backoff for noisy neighbours.\n                sleep(self.poll_every * 2).await;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn sign_payload(&self, envelope: &EventEnvelope) -> Result<String, OutboxError> {\n        let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(envelope)\n            .map_err(|e| OutboxError::Other(format!(\"serde: {e}\")))?;\n\n        // Sign the raw bytes (comment this if you prefer canonical JSON).\n        let sig = self.key_pair.sign(&mut bytes);\n        Ok(base64::encode(sig.as_ref()))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Example domain event.\n///\n/// In real code these live inside domain modules; kept here for demo.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct LessonPublished {\n    pub lesson_id: Uuid,\n    pub title:     String,\n    pub author_id: Uuid,\n    pub tags:      Vec<String>,\n}\n\nimpl DomainEvent   use ring::rand::SystemRandom;\n    use ring::signature::Ed25519KeyPair as Key;\n\n    if let Ok(pkcs8_b64) = std::env::var(\"EDUPULSE_SIGNING_KEY_B64\") {\n        let bytes = base64::decode(pkcs8_b64)\n            .map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?;\n        Key::from_pkcs8(&bytes).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)\n    } else {\n        // Auto-generate volatile key when none provided.\n        let rng = SystemRandom::new();\n        let pkcs8 = Key::generate_pkcs8(&rng).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?;\n        Ok(Key::from_pkcs8(pkcs8.as_ref()).map_err(|_| OutboxError::Crypto)?)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bootstraps a producer demo.\n///\n/// This async function illustrates how to embed the Outbox transaction\n/// into domain service logic. Reliable Domain Event Outbox implementation\n     5→//! ===========================================\n     6→//! This module provides a production-ready* implementation of the\n     7→//! Outbox/Inbox pattern for EduPulse Live.  All domain events are first\n     8→//! persisted inside the same Postgres transaction that modified the\n     9→//! aggregate and are later *atomically* published to the message broker\n    10→//! (e.g. RabbitMQ).  This guarantees *at-least-once* delivery without\n    11→//! sacrificing database consistency.\n    12→//!\n    13→//! # Architecture\n    14→//! 1.  batch_size: i64,\n   324→    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\n   325→    where\n   326→        P: EventPublisher,\n   327→    {\n   328→        // Fetch a batch; early return if empty\n   329→ // Retry with exponential back-off (max ~10 min)\n   344→            let mut backoff = ExponentialBackoff {\n   345→                initial_interval: Duration::from_millis(250),\n   346→                multiplier: 2.0,\n   347→                max_interval: Duration::from_secs(30),\n   348→                max_elapsed_time: Some(Duration::from_secs(600)),\n   349→
