Reliable Domain Event Outbox implementation\n//! ===========================================\n//! This module provides a production-ready* implementation of the\n//! Outbox/Inbox pattern for EduPulse Live.  All domain events are first\n//! persisted inside the same Postgres transaction that modified the\n//! aggregate and are later *atomically* published to the message broker\n//! (e.g. RabbitMQ).  This guarantees *at-least-once* delivery without\n//! sacrificing database consistency.\n//!\n//! # Architecture\n//! 1. Domain layer inserts a row into the `outbox_events` table\n//!    through [`OutboxRepository::insert`].\n//! 2. [`OutboxProcessor`] polls the table on an interval (or is triggered\n//!    by NOTIFY/LISTEN) and publishes unpublished events.\n//! 3. After a successful publish it marks the event as `published_at`.\n//!\n//! # Usage\n//! ```no_run\n//! #[tokio::main]\n//! async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n//!     env_logger::init();\n//!\n//!     let db = PgPoolOptions::new()\n//!         .max_connections(5)\n//!         .connect(\"postgres://user:password@localhost/edupulse\")\n//!         .await?;\n//!\n//!     // Initialise AMQP connection\n//!     let amqp_addr = \"amqp://guest:guest@127.0.0.1:5672/%2f\";\n//!     let publisher = RabbitMqPublisher::connect(amqp_addr, \"edupulse.events\").await?;\n//!\n//!     // Fire-and-forget background processing task\n//!     OutboxProcessor::spawn(db.clone(), publisher);\n//!\n//!     // … start REST API, etc.\n//!     Ok(())\n//! }\n//! ```\n//! *Error handling, retries, and back-off have been implemented but can be\n//! expanded with metrics, dead-letter queues, etc.*\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse backoff::{future::retry, ExponentialBackoff};\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse lapin::{\n    options::{BasicPublishOptions, QueueDeclareOptions},\n    types::FieldTable,\n    BasicProperties, Channel, Connection, ConnectionProperties,\n};\nuse log::{error, info, warn};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgRow, PgPool, Row};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    sync::Mutex,\n    task::{self, JoinHandle},\n    time,\n};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                Domain Event                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// A single row inside the `outbox_events` table.\n///\n/// NOTE: `payload` is stored as **raw JSON** to keep the table generic and\n/// future-proof.  Consumers can decode it based on the `event_type`.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, Clone)]\npub struct OutboxEvent {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub aggregate_type: String,\n    pub aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    pub event_type: String,\n    pub payload: serde_json::Value,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub published_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                  Errors                                    */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum OutboxError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Database(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"AMQP error: {0}\")]\n    Amqp(#[from] lapin::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),\n}\n\n/*   pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl OutboxRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n\n    /// Inserts a new [`OutboxEvent`] within an existing transaction or\n    /// standalone if `tx` is `None`.\n    pub async fn insert(\n        &self,\n        mut event: OutboxEvent,\n        tx: Option<&mut sqlx::Transaction<'_, sqlx::Postgres>>,\n    ) -> Result<OutboxEvent, OutboxError> {\n        event.id = event.id; // ensure id exists\n        let query = sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO outbox_events (\n                id,\n                aggregate_type,\n                aggregate_id,\n                event_type,\n                payload,\n                occurred_at,\n                published_at\n            ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7)\n            \"#,\n            event.id,\n            event.aggregate_type,\n            event.aggregate_id,\n            event.event_type,\n            event.payload,\n            event.occurred_at,\n            event.published_at\n        );\n\n        match tx {\n            Some(tx_ref) => {\n                query.execute(&mut *tx_ref).await?;\n            }\n            None => {\n                query.execute(&self.pool).await?;\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(event)\n    }\n\n    /// Fetches a batch of unpublished events, ordered by `occurred_at`.\n    pub async fn fetch_unpublished(\n        &self,\n        limit: i64,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<OutboxEvent>, OutboxError> {\n        let rows = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            SELECT\n                id,\n                aggregate_type,\n                aggregate_id,\n                event_type,\n                payload,\n                occurred_at,\n                published_at\n            FROM outbox_events\n            WHERE published_at IS NULL\n            ORDER BY occurred_at\n            LIMIT $1\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(limit)\n        .map(|row: PgRow| OutboxEvent {\n            id: row.get(\"id\"),\n            aggregate_type: row.get(\"aggregate_type\"),\n            aggregate_id: row.get(\"aggregate_id\"),\n            event_type: row.get(\"event_type\"),\n            payload: row.get::<serde_json::Value, _>(\"payload\"),\n            occurred_at: row.get(\"occurred_at\"),\n            published_at: row.get(\"published_at\"),\n        })\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rows)\n    }\n\n    /// Marks the event as published.\n    pub async fn mark_as_published(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            UPDATE outbox_events\n            SET published_at = NOW()\n            WHERE id = $1\n            \"#,\n            id\n        )\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                             Event Publisher                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Trait that abstracts away the underlying message broker implementation.\n/// This allows swapping RabbitMQ for NATS, Kafka, etc. without touching the\n/// domain/application layer.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync + 'static {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &OutboxEvent) -> Result<(), OutboxError>;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                             RabbitMQ Publisher                             */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Thin wrapper around a RabbitMQ channel that implements [`EventPublisher`].\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct RabbitMqPublisher {\n    channel: Arc<Channel>,\n    exchange: String,\n}\n\nimpl RabbitMqPublisher {\n    /// Establishes a connection and ensures that the declared exchange exists.\n    pub async fn connect(\n        amqp_addr: &str,\n        exchange: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Self, OutboxError> {\n        let conn = Connection::connect(amqp_addr, ConnectionProperties::default()).await?;\n        let channel = conn.create_channel().await?;\n\n        channel\n            .queue_declare(\n                exchange,\n                QueueDeclareOptions::default(),\n                FieldTable::default(),\n            )\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            channel: Arc::new(channel),\n            exchange: exchange.to_string(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RabbitMqPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &OutboxEvent) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event)?;\n        self.channel\n            .basic_publish(\n                \"\",\n                &self.exchange,\n                BasicPublishOptions::default(),\n                &payload,\n                BasicProperties::default()\n                    .with_content_type(\"application/json\".into())\n                    .with_message_id(event.id.to_string().into())\n                    .with_type(event.event_type.clone().into()),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await?; // wait for broker confirmation\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                            Outbox Processor                                */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n/// Responsible for periodically polling the DB, publishing events and\n/// updating their status. It runs indefinitely in a tokio task.\npub struct OutboxProcessor {\n    handle: JoinHandle<()>,\n}\n\nimpl OutboxProcessor {\n    /// Spawns the background `OutboxProcessor` task with sensible defaults.\n    ///\n    /// `poll_interval` defaults to 500 ms and `batch_size` to 100.\n    pub fn spawn<P>(pool: PgPool, publisher: P) -> Self\n    where\n        P: EventPublisher,\n    {\n        let poll_interval = Duration::from_millis(500);\n        let batch_size = 100;\n\n        let repo = OutboxRepository::new(pool);\n        let publisher = Arc::new(publisher);\n\n        let handle = task::spawn(async move {\n            let mut interval = time::interval(poll_interval);\n            loop {\n                interval.tick().await;\n                if let Err(err) =\n                    Self::drain_batch(&repo, Arc::clone(&publisher), batch_size).await\n                {\n                    error!(\"Error draining outbox: {:?}\", err);\n                }\n            }\n        });\n\n        Self { handle }\n    }\n\n    async fn drain_batch<P>(\n        repo: &OutboxRepository,\n        publisher: Arc<P>,\n        batch_size: i64,\n    ) -> Result<(), OutboxError>\n    where\n        P: EventPublisher,\n    {\n        // Fetch a batch; early return if empty\n        let events = repo.fetch_unpublished(batch_size).await?;\n        if events.is_empty() {\n            return Ok(());\n        }\n\n        // Publish events sequentially (could be parallel with `join_all` if\n        // ordering isn't important)\n        for event in events {\n            let op = || async {\n                publisher.publish(&event).await?;\n                repo.mark_as_published(event.id).await?;\n                Ok::<(), OutboxError>(())\n            };\n\n            // Retry with exponential back-off (max ~10 min)\n            let mut backoff = ExponentialBackoff {\n                initial_interval: Duration::from_millis(250),\n                multiplier: 2.0,\n                max_interval: Duration::from_secs(30),\n                max_elapsed_time: Some(Duration::from_secs(600)),\n                ..Default::default()\n            };\n\n            if let Err(err) = retry(backoff, op).await {\n                // This should rarely happen; send to Sentry, Dead-letter, etc.\n                warn!(\"Failed to publish event {}: {:?}\", event.id, err);\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Waits for the background task to finish (e.g. on graceful shutdown).\n    pub async fn join(self) -> Result<(), task::JoinError> {\n        self.handle.await\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                   Tests                                    */\n/* }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Repository Layer ---------- */\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait PulseRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn insert(&self, pulse: Pulse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError>;\n    async fn {\n    #[error(\"database unavailable\")]\n    Unavailable,\n    #[error(\"anyhow: {0}\")]\n    Other(#[from] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n/// A simple, thread-safe in-memory implementation useful for unit tests.\n/// Not optimized for production—replace with Postgres, MongoDB, etc.\n#[derive(Default)]\npub struct InMemoryPulseRepository {\n    store: parking_lot::RwLock<std::collections::HashMap<PulseId, Pulse>>,\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl PulseRepository for InMemoryPulseRepository {\n    async fn insert(&self, pulse: Pulse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError> {\n        let mut guard = self.store.write();\n        guard.insert(pulse.id, pulse);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    async fn find(&self, id: PulseId) -> Result<Option<Pulse>, RepositoryError> {\n        let guard = self.store.read();\n        Ok(guard.get(&id).cloned())\n    }\n\n    async fn upsert(&self, pulse: Pulse) -> Result<(), RepositoryError> {\n        let mut guard = self.store.write();\n        guard.insert(pulse.id, pulse);\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Message Broker Abstraction ---------- */\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Event store responsible for persisting `EventEnvelope`s in DB.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventStore {\n    async fn append(&self, events: &[EventEnvelope]) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error>;\n}\n\n/// Publisher responsible for pushing events to the broker.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, events: &[EventEnvelope]) -> Result<(), PublishError>;\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Concrete Implementations\n// sqlx::Error> {\n        sqlx::query!(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO event_store (\n                id,\n                sequence,\n                aggregate_id,\n                aggregate_type,\n                event,\n                occurred_at\n            ) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6)\n            \"#,\n            envelope.id,\n            envelope.sequence,\n            envelope.aggregate_id,\n            envelope.aggregate_type,\n            serde_json::to_value(&envelope.event).expect(\"serialisation never fails\"),\n            envelope.occurred_at\n        )\n        .execute(&mut *tx)\n        .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventStore for PostgresEventStore {\n    #[instrument(skip_all, name = \"event_store.append\")]\n    async fn append(&self, events: &[EventEnvelope]) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error> {\n        let mut tx = self.pool.begin().await?;\n        for ev in events {\n            Self::append_single(&mut tx, ev).await?;\n        }\n        tx.commit().await\n    }\n}\n\n/// Dummy publisher; swap with NATS / RabbitMQ / Kafka implementation.\npub struct LogPublisher;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher info!(event_id = %ev.id, \"published event to broker\");\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Service Layer\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\npub struct GdprService<S, P>\nwhere\n    S: EventStore + Send + Sync,\n    P: EventPublisher + Send + Sync,\n{\n    store: S,\n    publisher: P,\n}\n\nimpl<S, P> GdprService<S, P>\nwhere\n    S: EventStore + Send + Sync,\n    P: EventPublisher + Send + Sync,\n{\n    pub fn new(store: S, publisher: P) -> Self {\n        Self { store, publisher }\n    }\n\n    /// Dispatch a GDPR command, storing and emitting the resulting events.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, cmd))]\n    pub async fn handle(&self, cmd: GdprCommand) -> Result<(), GdprError> {\n        // Step 1 – Translate command → events\n        let events = self.to_events(cmd);\n\n        // Step 2 – Persist events inside a DB transaction\n self.publisher.publish(&events).await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    fn to_events(&self, cmd: GdprCommand) -> Vec<EventEnvelope> {\n        match cmd {\n            GdprCommand::EraseUser {\n                requesting_admin_id,\n                target_user_id,\n                reason,\n            } => {\n                let base_seq = self.sequence_seed();\n                vec![self.envelope(\n                    base_seq,\n                    target_user_id,\n                    DomainEvent::UserErasureRequested {\n                        target_user_id,\n                        requesting_admin_id,\n                        reason,\n                    },\n                )]\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn sequence_seed(&self) -> i64 {\n        // In production fn envelope(&self, seq: i64, aggregate_id: Uuid, ev: DomainEvent) -> EventEnvelope {\n        EventEnvelope {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            sequence: seq,\n            aggregate_id,\n            aggregate_type: \"user\".to_string(),\n            event: ev,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// This module contains everything required to create, persist, and dispatch\n//! immutable domain events in a fully-async, **Tokio** powered context.  The\n//! code is designed to be reusable across bounded contexts and hides the\n//! mechanics of the message broker behind a lightweight `EventBus` façade.\n//!\n//! Production-quality concerns included:\n//!   • Proper error propagation (`thiserror`)  \n//!   • Database integration (`sqlx` w/ compile-time SQL checks)  \n//!   • Structured, versioned events with serialization guards (`serde`)  \n//!   • Broadcast-based in-process fan-out with back-pressure awareness  \n//!   • Cancellation-safe, async/await-friendly API\n//!\n//! NOTE: In production, the `EventBus` would be backed by Kafka/RabbitMQ/Redis.\n//! /// Schema version for data migrations / evolution.\n    pub version:      i32,\n}\n\nimpl EventEnvelope {\n    /// Creates an envelope from any `DomainEvent` implementor.\n    pub fn new<E: DomainEvent>(event: &E, correlation: Option<Uuid>) -> Result<Self, EventError> {\n        let payload = serde_json::to_value(event)\n            .map_err(EventError::Serialization)?;\n        Ok(Self {\n            id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n            event_type: event.event_type().to_owned(),\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n            correlation,\n            payload,\n            version: Persists immutable events in an append-only table.\n///\n/// SQL:\n/// ```sql\n/// CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event_store (\n///     id            UUID PRIMARY KEY,\n///     event_type    TEXT NOT NULL,\n///     occurred_at   TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,\n///     correlation   UUID NULL,\n///     payload       JSONB NOT NULL,\n///     version       INT  NOT NULL\n/// );\n/// ```\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct PgEventStore {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgEventStore {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n\n    /// Persist a single envelope inside a transactional correlation, payload, version)\",\n        )\n        .push_values(\n            std::iter::once(env),\n            |mut b, e| {\n                b.push_bind(e.id)\n                    .push_bind(&e.event_type)\n                    .push_bind(e.occurred_at)\n                    .push_bind(e.correlation)\n                    .push_bind(&e.payload)\n   teacher_id: Uuid::nil(),\n                title: String::new(),\n            }) {\n                // Deserialize lazily to avoid unnecessary allocations.\n                match       }\n                    Err(err) => {\n                        tracing::error!(error = %err, \"failed to deserialize LessonPublished\");\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    })\n}\n\n/// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n/// Integration test (requires `sqlx` offline mode OR live database)\n/// Event-sourcing audit-trail and GDPR-erase utilities used by several\n//! EduPulse Live services.  The module is designed so that other crates\n//! (web API, background workers, analytics pipeline, …) can depend on a\n//! single abstraction while swapping out storage back-ends if necessary.\n//!\n//! Compile with the following feature flags enabled in `Cargo.toml`:\n//!\n//! ```toml\n//! [dependencies]\n//! tokio      = { version = \"1\", features = [\"rt-multi-thread\", \"macros\"] }\n//! sqlx       = { version = \"0.7\", features = [\"runtime-tokio-rustls\", \"postgres\", \"chrono\", \"uuid\", \"json\"] }\n//! serde      = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n//! serde_json = \"1\"\n//! chrono     = { version = \"0.4\", features = [\"serde\"] }\n//! uuid       = { version = \"1\", features = [\"serde\", \"v4\"] }\n//! async-trait = \"0.1\"\n//! anyhow     = \"1\"\n//! thiserror  = \"1\"\n//! tracing    = \"0.1\"\n//! ```\n//!\n//! The trait is intentionally narrow\n/// so that adapters for DynamoDB, Kafka-compacted topics, or filesystem\n/// snapshots can be added later with minimal friction.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventStore: Send + Sync {\n    async fn append_event(&self, event: &AuditEvent) -> Result<(), EventStoreError>;\n    async fn fetch_events_by_aggregate(\n        &self,\n        aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    ) -> Result<Vec<AuditEvent>, EventStoreError>;\n    /// GDPR-style erase.  The default behaviour is pseudonymisation:\n    /// personal fields in the JSON payload are stripped/replaced while\n    /// keeping aggregate-level metadata for analytics/regulatory audits.\n    async fn pseudonymise_user(\n        &self,\n        user_id: Uuid,\n        pseudonym: &str,\n    ) -> Result<(), EventStoreError>;\n}\n\n/// Postgres 15 implementation. Callers can also pass a pre-initialised `PgPool`\n    /// obtained from an application-wide connection manager.\n    pub async fn new(pool: Option<PgPool>) -> Result<Self, EventStoreError> {\n .bind(event.created_at)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        info!(%event.event_id, \"event persisted\");\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all, created_at ASC\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(aggregate_id)\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        let events = rows.into_iter().map(row_to_event).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;\n        Ok(events)\n    }\n\n    /// Iterates through *all* events where `aggregate_id = user_id`, then\n    /// transforms the JSON      .await?;\n\n        for row in rows {\n            let mut event = row_to_event(row)?;\n            event.payload = pseudonymise_payload(&event.payload, pseudonym)?;\n            sqlx::query(\n                r#\"\n                UPDATE audit_events\n                SET payload = $1\n                WHERE event_id = $2\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(&event.payload)\n            .bind(event.event_id)\n            .execute(&mut *tx)\n            .await?;\n        }\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        info!(%user_id, \"pseudonymisation complete ({} events)\", row.try_get(\"event_id\")?,\n        aggregate_id: row.try_get(\"aggregate_id\")?,\n        event_type: row.try_get(\"event_type\")?,\n        payload: row.try_get(\"payload\")?,\n        created_at: row.try_get(\"created_at\")?,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Recurse through JSON to strip direct identifiers (name, email, …) and\n/// replace them with a constant pseudonym.  This is obviously simplistic,\n/// but demonstrates the technique.  Real-world code would leverage schema\n/// introspection or build explicit per-event transformers.\nfn pseudonymise_payload(\n    original: &JsonValue,\n    pseudonym: &str,\n) -> Result<JsonValue, EventStoreError> {\n    let mut clone = original.clone();\n\n    match &mut clone {\n        JsonValue::Object(map) => {\n            static SENSITIVE_KEYS: &[&str] = &[\"name\", \"email\", \"first_name\", \"last_name\"];\n            for key in SENSITIVE_KEYS {\n                if map.contains_key(*key) {\n                    map.insert((*key).to_string(), JsonValue::String(pseudonym.to_owned()));\n                }\n            }\n            // Recurse into nested values\n            for v in map.values_mut() {\n                *v = pseudonymise_payload(v, pseudonym)?;\n            }\n        }\n        JsonValue::Array(arr) => {\n            for v in arr.iter_mut() {\n                *v = pseudonymise_payload(v, pseudonym)?;\n            }\n        }\n        _ => { /* primitives stay unchanged */ }\n    }\n    Ok(clone)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use serde_json::json;\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_pseudonymise_payload() {\n        let original = json!({\n            \"name\": \"Alice\",\n            \"email\": \"alice@example.com\",\n            \"score\": 95,\n            \"nested\": {\n                \"first_name\": \"Alice\",\n                \"comments\": [\"great job\"]\n            }\n        });\n\n        let transformed = pseudonymise_payload(&original, \"redacted\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"name\"], \"redacted\");\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"nested\"][\"first_name\"], \"redacted\");\n        assert_eq!(transformed[\"score\"], 95);  producer: FutureProducer,\n    consumer: StreamConsumer,\n}\n\nimpl KafkaEventBus {\n    /// Build from environment variables (see `README.md`).\n    ///\n    /// Required:\n    /// - error!(err = %e, \"Failed to persist audit event\");\n                    }\n                }\n                Err(e) => error!(err = %e, \"Error while reading from event stream\"),\n            }\n The code demonstrates several architectural concepts used across\n//! the code-base: Service layer, Repository pattern, event emission,\n//! and async database / broker integrations.\n//!\n//! ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n//! │ Teacher creates a Pulse (REST endpoint → Service)│\n//! └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n//!                    │\n//!            +-------▼---------+\n//!            | PulseService     |\n//!            +------------------+\n//!   ┌create─►| Repo.create()    |────────────┐\n//!   │        |                  |            │\n//!   │        | Publisher.publish(event)      │\n//!   │        +------------------+            │\n//!   │                    │                  ▼\n//!   │          +---------▼--------+  +---------------+\n//!   │          | Postgres ledger  |  | RabbitMQ/Kafka|\n//!   │          +------------------+  +---------------+\n//!   │                                          │\n//!   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘\n//!\n//! Down-stream workers subscribe to PulseCreated events to perform\n//! search indexing, scoring bootstrap, notification fan-out, etc.\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse lapin::{\n    options::BasicPublishOptions,\n    BasicProperties, Channel, ExchangeKind,\n};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgQueryResult, PgPool, Postgres, QueryBuilder};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::time::timeout;\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Domain model for a “Learning Pulse”\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LearningPulse {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub title: String,\n    pub description: String,\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub created_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub expires_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/// DTO required to create a new Pulse\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct NewPulse<'a> {\n    pub title: &'a str,\n    pub description: &'a str,\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub expires_at: Option<DateTime<Utc>>,\n}\n\n/// Event envelope published to the broker.\n///\n/// In real production code we would version this schema and add tracing\n/// correlation identifiers; for the sake of brevity we only include the\n/// basics here.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub enum DomainEvent {\n    PulseCreated(PulseCreated),\n}\n\n/// Payload for the PulseCreated event\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct PulseCreated {\n    pub pulse_id: Uuid,\n    pub teacher_id: Uuid,\n    pub occurred_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// Custom error hierarchy for the module\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum PulseError {\n    #[error(\"database error: {0}\")]\n    Database(#[from] sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"event broker error: {0}\")]\n    Broker(#[from] lapin::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"pulse not found (id = {0})\")]\n    NotFound(Uuid),\n\n    #[error(\"operation timed out\")]\n    Timeout,\n}\n\n/// Repository abstraction\n#[async_trait]\npub trait PulseRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn create(&self, new_pulse: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError>;\n    async fn find_by_id(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError>;\n}\n\n/// Event publisher abstraction\n#[async_trait]\npub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<(), PulseError>;\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                         Postgres-backed Repository                         */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\npub struct PgPulseRepository {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgPulseRepository {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl PulseRepository for PgPulseRepository {\n    async fn create(&self, new_pulse: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        // SQLx query builder gives us compile-time checked SQL.\n        let mut builder: QueryBuilder<Postgres> =\n            QueryBuilder::new(\"INSERT INTO learning_pulses \");\n        builder.push(\"(id, title, description, teacher_id, created_at, expires_at)\")\n            .push(\" VALUES (\")\n            .push_bind(Uuid::new_v4())\n            .push(\", \")\n            .push_bind(new_pulse.title)\n            .push(\", \")\n            .push_bind(new_pulse.description)\n            .push(\", \")\n            .push_bind(new_pulse.teacher_id)\n            .push(\", \")\n            .push_bind(Utc::now())\n            .push(\", \")\n            .push_bind(new_pulse.expires_at)\n            .push(\") RETURNING id, title, description, teacher_id, created_at, expires_at\");\n\n        let pulse = builder\n            .build_query_as::<LearningPulse>()\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n\n    async fn find_by_id(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        let pulse = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LearningPulse,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, title, description, teacher_id, created_at, expires_at\n            FROM learning_pulses\n            WHERE id = $1\n            \"#,\n            id\n        )\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        pulse.ok_or(PulseError::NotFound(id))\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                          RabbitMQ Event Publisher                          */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\nconst DEFAULT_EXCHANGE: &str = \"edu_pulse_domain_events\";\n\npub struct RabbitMqPublisher {\n    channel: Channel,\n}\n\nimpl RabbitMqPublisher {\n    pub fn new(channel: Channel) -> Self {\n        Self { channel }\n    }\n\n    async fn ensure_exchange(&self) -> Result<(), lapin::Error> {\n        self.channel\n            .exchange_declare(\n                DEFAULT_EXCHANGE,\n                ExchangeKind::Topic,\n                lapin::options::ExchangeDeclareOptions {\n                    durable: true,\n                    ..Default::default()\n                },\n                Default::default(),\n            )\n            .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EventPublisher for RabbitMqPublisher {\n    async fn publish(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<(), PulseError> {\n        // Lazily declare the exchange. If it already exists,\n        // RabbitMQ will simply acknowledge.\n        self.ensure_exchange().await?;\n\n        let routing_key = match event {\n            DomainEvent::PulseCreated(_) => \"pulse.created\",\n        };\n\n        let payload = serde_json::to_vec(event).expect(\"serialization never fails\");\n\n        self.channel\n            .basic_publish(\n                DEFAULT_EXCHANGE,\n                routing_key,\n                BasicPublishOptions::default(),\n                &payload,\n                BasicProperties::default().with_content_type(\"application/json\".into()),\n            )\n            .await?\n            .await?; // Wait for broker confirmation.\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                              Service Layer                                 */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\npub struct PulseService<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> {\n    repo: R,\n    publisher: P,\n    broker_timeout: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> PulseService<R, P> {\n    pub fn new(repo: R, publisher: P) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            publisher,\n            broker_timeout: Duration::from_secs(5),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Create a Pulse and broadcast a `PulseCreated` domain event.\n    ///\n    /// The method is transactional at the application level. If the event\n    /// cannot be published within [`broker_timeout`], the database insert is\n    /// **not** rolled back (best-effort), but the caller is informed through\n    /// an error.\n    pub async fn create_pulse(&self, new: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        let pulse = self.repo.create(new).await?;\n\n        let event = DomainEvent::PulseCreated(PulseCreated {\n            pulse_id: pulse.id,\n            teacher_id: pulse.teacher_id,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        });\n\n        // We don't want to await forever; use `tokio::time::timeout`.\n        timeout(self.broker_timeout, self.publisher.publish(&event))\n            .await\n            .map_err(|_| PulseError::Timeout)??;\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n\n    pub async fn get(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        self.repo.find_by_id(id).await\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n/*                                   Tests                                    */\n/* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- */\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use std::sync::Arc;\n    use tokio::sync::Mutex;\n\n    /// In-memory mock repository for unit tests\n    struct InMemoryRepo {\n        inner: Arc<Mutex<Vec<LearningPulse>>>,\n    }\n\n    impl InMemoryRepo {\n        fn new() -> Self {\n            Self {\n                inner: Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new())),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    impl PulseRepository for InMemoryRepo {\n        async fn create(&self, new_pulse: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n            let pulse = LearningPulse {\n                id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n                title: new_pulse.title.to_string(),\n                description: new_pulse.description.to_string(),\n                teacher_id: new_pulse.teacher_id,\n                created_at: Utc::now(),\n                expires_at: new_pulse.expires_at,\n            };\n            self.inner.lock().await.push(pulse.clone());\n            Ok(pulse)\n        }\n\n        async fn find_by_id(&self, id: Uuid) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n            let vec = self.inner.lock().await;\n            vec.iter()\n                .find(|p| p.id == id)\n                .cloned()\n                .ok_or(PulseError::NotFound(id))\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// In-memory publisher that records outgoing events\n    struct VecPublisher {\n        events: Arc<Mutex<Vec<DomainEvent>>>,\n    }\n\n    impl VecPublisher {\n        fn new() -> Self {\n            Self {\n                events: Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new())),\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[async_trait]\n    impl EventPublisher for VecPublisher {\n        async fn publish(&self, event: &DomainEvent) -> Result<(), PulseError> {\n            self.events.lock().await.push(event.clone());\n            Ok(())\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_create_pulse_emits_event() {\n        let repo = InMemoryRepo::new();\n        let publisher = VecPublisher::new();\n        let service = PulseService::new(repo, publisher.clone());\n\n        let teacher_id = Uuid::new_v4();\n        let new = NewPulse {\n            title: \"Test Pulse\",\n            description: \"Describe the mitochondria.\",\n            teacher_id,\n            expires_at: None,\n        };\n\n        let pulse = service.create_pulse(&new).await.unwrap();\n\n        // Verify pulse fields\n        assert_eq!(pulse.title, new.title);\n        // Verify event dispatch\n        let events = publisher.events.lock().await;\n        assert_eq!(events.len(), 1);\n        matches!(events[0], DomainEvent::PulseCreated(_));\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":374,"startLine":1,"totalLines":374}}}

The platform follows a CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) architecture with an event-sourcing pattern. After thorough analysis of the codebase, I have identified the core architectural components, traced a complete data flow, and developed a strategic integration plan for the proposed Real-time Collaborative Whiteboard feature.\n\n---\n\n## Key Files Identified\n\n### Configuration and Infrastructure\n- `/app/project/src/config.txt` - **AuthenticationService** - JWT-based authentication with Redis session management, bcrypt password hashing, and role-based access control\n\n### Event Bus Infrastructure\n- `/app/project/src/module_4.txt` - **EventPublisher & EventStore** - Core event infrastructure using NATS JetStream with event sourcing\n- `/app/project/src/module_9.txt` - **Outbox Pattern Implementation** - Reliable event publishing with Ed25519 signatures and NATS broker\n- `/app/project/src/module_12.txt` - **Event Bus Abstraction** - NATS-backed event bus with in-process fan-out and back-pressure awareness\n- `/app/project/src/module_50.txt` - **Outbox Processor** - RabbitMQ/AMQP event publishing with exponential backoff retries\n\n### Domain Services (Write Model)\n- `/app/project/src/module_33.txt` - **PulseService** - Learning Pulse domain with Service/Repository/EventPublisher pattern\n- `/app/project/src/module_60.txt` - **PulseService (Alternative)** - Business logic for creating learning pulses and handling student replies\n- `/app/project/src/module_81.txt` - **LearningPulseService** - Domain model with validation, event generation, and persistence\n\n### Event Consumers (Read Model / CQRS Projections)\n- `/app/project/src/module_15.txt` - **Badge Awarding Service** - Event consumer that awards badges based on domain events\n- `/app/project/src/module_27.txt` - **Search Indexing Service** - Event consumer that indexes pulses into Elasticsearch\n- `/app/project/src/module_20.txt` - **EventDispatcher** - Combines event store persistence with in-process fan-out\n\n### Event Infrastructure (Multiple Broker Support)\n- `/app/project/src/module_64.txt` - **Kafka EventBus** - Kafka-backed event bus with audit trail consumer\n- `/app/project/src/module_72.txt` - **NATS EventPublisher** - Domain event infrastructure with typed events and NATS publishing\n\n### GDPR & Compliance\n- `/app/project/src/module_45.txt` - **GDPR Erasure Service** - Event-sourced user data erasure with soft-delete events\n- `/app/project/src/module_6.txt` - **Audit Event Store** - GDPR-compliant event store with pseudonymization support\n\n---\n\n## Code Evidence\n\n### 1. Authentication & Session Management (module_1.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_1.txt\n// Lines: 14-27 (Service Description)\n//!\n//! EduPulse Live – Session-based Authentication Service.\n//!\n//! Responsibilities:\n//! ● Secure user registration (bcrypt password hashing)\n//! ● JWT-based authentication with refresh tokens\n//! ● Session storage in Redis with configurable TTL\n//! ● Role-based access control (RBAC)\n//!\n//! The module exposes an HTTP middleware and a standalone service\n//! that can be used from Axum, Actix-web or any other async framework.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_1.txt\n// Lines: 133-157 (JWT Authentication)\npub async fn login(\n    &self,\n    email: &str,\n    password_raw: &str,\n) -> Result<AuthResponse, AuthError> {\n    // 1. Find user by email\n    let user = self\n        .repo\n        .find_by_email(email)\n        .await?\n        .ok_or(AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n    // 2. Verify password hash (bcrypt)\n    bcrypt::verify(password_raw, &user.password_hash)?;\n\n    // 3. Mint JWT tokens (access + refresh)\n    let access_token = self.mint_access_token(&user)?;\n    let refresh_token = self.mint_refresh_token(&user)?;\n\n    // 4. Create a server-side session in Redis\n    let session = Session::new(user.id, user.role.clone(), self.session_ttl);\n    self.sessions.set(&session).await?;\n\n    Ok(AuthResponse {\n        access_token,\n        refresh_token,\n        session_id: session.id,\n    })\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Event Publishing Infrastructure (module_4.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_4.txt\n// Lines: 1-20 (Module Description)\n//! src/module_4.rs\n//!\n//! EduPulse Live – Event-Sourcing Core\n//!\n//! This module provides the building blocks for implementing event-sourced\n//! aggregates and reliable message publishing within the \"EduPulse Live\"\n//! platform.  Key components:\n//!\n//! ● `DomainEvent` – trait marking an event eligible for persistence/publishing\n//! ● `EventStore` – trait for persisting events (Postgres implementation)\n//! ● `EventPublisher` – trait for pushing events to the message bus (NATS impl)\n//! ● `OutboxForwarder` – worker that drains the \"outbox\" table and publishes\n//!\n//! The design follows the Outbox Pattern to guarantee *exactly-once* delivery\n//! even when the primary DB and the message broker are separate systems.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_4.txt\n// Lines: 240-268 (NATS JetStream Publisher)\npub struct NatsJsEventPublisher {\n    nc: Arc<nats::asynk::Connection>,\n    jetstream: async_nats::jetstream::Context,\n}\n\nimpl NatsJsEventPublisher {\n    pub async fn connect(url: &str, stream_name: &str) -> Result<Self, PublishError> {\n        let nc = nats::asynk::connect(url)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| PublishError::Connection(e.to_string()))?;\n        let js = async_nats::jetstream::new(nc.clone());\n\n        // Ensure stream exists with sensible defaults\n        js.get_or_create_stream(async_nats::jetstream::stream::Config {\n            name: stream_name.to_string(),\n            subjects: vec![format!(\"{stream_name}.>\")],\n            max_age: Duration::from_secs(60 * 60 * 24 * 7), // 7 days\n            ..Default::default()\n        })\n        .await\n        .map_err(|e| PublishError::JetStream(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            nc: Arc::new(nc),\n            jetstream: js,\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Service Layer Pattern (module_33.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_33.txt\n// Lines: 238-280 (Service Layer)\npub struct PulseService<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> {\n    repo: R,\n    publisher: P,\n    broker_timeout: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> PulseService<R, P> {\n    /// Create a Pulse and broadcast a `PulseCreated` domain event.\n    ///\n    /// The method is transactional at the application level. If the event\n    /// cannot be published within [`broker_timeout`], the database insert is\n    /// **not** rolled back (best-effort), but the caller is informed through\n    /// an error.\n    pub async fn create_pulse(&self, new: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        let pulse = self.repo.create(new).await?;\n\n        let event = DomainEvent::PulseCreated(PulseCreated {\n            pulse_id: pulse.id,\n            teacher_id: pulse.teacher_id,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        });\n\n        // We don't want to await forever; use `tokio::time::timeout`.\n        timeout(self.broker_timeout, self.publisher.publish(&event))\n            .await\n            .map_err(|_| PulseError::Timeout)??;\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Event Consumer Pattern (module_15.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_15.txt\n// Lines: 1-25 (Event Consumer Service)\n//! src/module_15.rs\n//!\n//! Badge-Awarding Worker\n//! ---------------------\n//! This module implements a background worker that listens to domain events and\n//! awards badges to users when they satisfy certain achievement criteria.  It\n//! showcases:\n//!  • Event-driven, fully asynchronous architecture\n//!  • Clean separation of Event-handling / Repository / Service concerns\n//!  • Structured logging via `tracing`\n//!  • Strong typing for event payloads\n//!\n//! In production this worker would be deployed as a separate binary or k8s job,\n//! connected to the message broker (NATS / RabbitMQ / Kafka).  For testability\n//! the broker abstraction is hidden behind an `EventConsumer` trait.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_15.txt\n// Lines: 180-210 (Event Handling Logic)\nasync fn handle_event(\n    &self,\n    envelope: EventEnvelope<'_>,\n) -> Result<(), WorkerError> {\n    match &envelope.payload {\n        DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted {\n            student_id,\n            score,\n            ..\n        } if *score >= 90.0 => {\n            // Award \"Quiz Ace\" badge\n            let badge = Badge::new(\n                *student_id,\n                BadgeKind::QuizAce,\n                \"Scored ≥90% on a quiz\",\n            );\n            self.badge_repo.save(badge).await?;\n            info!(user_id = %student_id, \"Awarded QuizAce badge\");\n        }\n        DomainEvent::PeerFeedbackGiven {\n            reviewer_id, ..\n        } => {\n            // Award \"Helpful Peer\" badge after 5 feedbacks\n            let count = self.badge_repo.count_by_user(*reviewer_id).await?;\n            if count >= 5 {\n                let badge = Badge::new(*reviewer_id, BadgeKind::HelpfulPeer, \"Gave 5+ peer feedbacks\");\n                self.badge_repo.save(badge).await?;\n            }\n        }\n        _ => {}\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n### 5. CQRS Evidence - Configuration with Multiple DB URLs\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n// Lines: 63-69 (Database Configuration)\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct DatabaseConfig {\n    pub driver: String,\n    pub url: String,\n    pub pool_size: u32,\n}\n```\n\n```yaml\n# File: /app/project/src/config.txt (Example Configuration)\n# Lines: 22-52\ndatabase:\n  driver: \"postgres\"\n  url: \"postgres://epulse:secret@db/edupulse_live\"\n  pool_size: 30\nbroker:\n  host: \"rabbitmq\"\n  port: 5672\n  username: \"mq_user\"\n  password: \"mq_pass\"\n  vhost: \"/edupulse\"\nserver:\n  redis_uri: \"redis://redis:6379\"\n  redis_session_ttl_secs: 86_400\nsearch:\n  elasticsearch_url: \"http://elasticsearch:9200\"\n  index_prefix: \"edupulse_live_\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Core Architectural Components Identification\n\nBased on comprehensive code analysis, the EduPulse Live platform implements the following architectural patterns:\n\n| Component Role | Module(s) | Evidence |\n|---------------|-----------|----------|\n| **Authentication & Session** | `module_1.txt` | JWT tokens, bcrypt hashing, Redis session store, RBAC |\n| **Event Bus / Message Broker** | `module_4.txt`, `module_12.txt`, `module_64.txt`, `module_72.txt` | NATS JetStream, RabbitMQ (lapin), Kafka (rdkafka) implementations |\n| **Command Handlers (Write Model)** | `module_33.txt`, `module_60.txt`, `module_81.txt` | PulseService with Repository + EventPublisher pattern |\n| **Event Consumers (Read Model)** | `module_15.txt`, `module_27.txt`, `module_20.txt` | Badge awarding, Search indexing, Audit trail consumers |\n| **Outbox Pattern** | `module_9.txt`, `module_50.txt` | Reliable at-least-once event delivery with database-backed outbox |\n| **Event Store** | `module_4.txt`, `module_45.txt`, `module_6.txt` | PostgreSQL event store with append-only semantics |\n\n### 2. Architectural Patterns Identified\n\n#### Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)\nThe system uses an event-driven architecture where domain changes are captured as immutable events and published to a message broker for downstream processing.\n\n**Evidence:**\n- Multiple `EventPublisher` trait implementations (NATS, RabbitMQ, Kafka)\n- `DomainEvent` enums with typed event variants (`PulseCreated`, `QuizSubmitted`, `BadgeAwarded`)\n- Event envelope wrappers with correlation IDs and metadata\n- Asynchronous event consumers for search indexing and badge awarding\n\n#### CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation)\nThe system separates write operations (commands) from read operations (queries), with events bridging the two models.\n\n**Evidence:**\n- Write model: `PulseService` handles commands like `create_pulse()` and persists to primary database\n- Read model: `SearchIndexer` (module_27) maintains Elasticsearch index for optimized queries\n- Event-driven synchronization: Events flow from write services to read model consumers\n- Separate infrastructure: PostgreSQL for writes, Elasticsearch for search queries, Redis for sessions\n\n#### Outbox Pattern\nTo ensure reliable event delivery even when the database and message broker are separate systems, the platform uses the Outbox pattern.\n\n**Evidence from module_9.txt:**\n```rust\n/// Outbox service coordinates persistence and dispatching.\npub struct OutboxService<R: OutboxRepository + Send + Sync> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    nats_url: String,\n    key_pair: Arc<Ed25519KeyPair>,\n}\n\n/// Persists a domain event within an existing transaction.\npub async fn stage_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n    &self,\n    tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n    aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    event: &E,\n) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n    // Event is persisted in the same transaction as the domain change\n    self.repo.insert(tx, &unsigned).await\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Data Flow Analysis: Creating a Post/Pulse in a Study Group\n\nBased on the codebase analysis, here is the complete lifecycle of a user creating a new \"Learning Pulse\" (equivalent to a post in a study group):\n\n### Step-by-Step Flow\n\n1. **API Request (HTTP Layer)**\n   - User sends authenticated POST request to create a pulse\n   - JWT token validated by `module_1.txt` (AuthenticationService)\n   - Session verified against Redis\n\n2. **Command Processing (module_33.txt / module_81.txt)**\n   - `PulseService.create_pulse()` receives the command\n   - Domain validation performed (non-empty title, description)\n   - `LearningPulse` aggregate created with new UUID\n\n3. **Persistence (Repository Layer)**\n   - `PgPulseRepository.create()` inserts record into PostgreSQL\n   - Uses SQLx with compile-time query checking\n\n4. **Event Generation**\n   - `PulseCreated` domain event generated with metadata\n   - Event wrapped in `EventEnvelope` with correlation ID\n\n5. **Event Publishing (module_4.txt / module_9.txt)**\n   - Event staged in outbox table within same transaction (Outbox Pattern)\n   - `OutboxProcessor` polls for unpublished events\n   - Event published to NATS/RabbitMQ with routing key `pulse.created`\n\n6. **Event Consumption (module_27.txt / module_15.txt)**\n   - `SearchIndexer` receives `PulsePublished` event\n   - Fetches full pulse data from repository\n   - Indexes document into Elasticsearch for search queries\n   - Badge service checks if user qualifies for achievements\n\n### Sequence of Modules Involved\n\n```\nClient Request\n    │\n    ▼\n[module_1.txt] Authentication/Session Validation\n    │\n    ▼\n[module_33.txt or module_81.txt] PulseService.create_pulse()\n    │\n    ├──▶ [PostgreSQL] Insert learning_pulses record\n    │\n    └──▶ [module_9.txt or module_50.txt] Outbox Event Staging\n              │\n              ▼\n         [module_4.txt or module_12.txt] Event Publication to NATS/RabbitMQ\n              │\n              ├──▶ [module_27.txt] Search Indexer → Elasticsearch\n              │\n              └──▶ [module_15.txt] Badge Worker → Award badges\n```\n\n---\n\n## Proposed Integration Strategy: Real-time Collaborative Whiteboard\n\n### High-Level Architecture\n\nThe proposed architecture introduces a dedicated **Whiteboard Service** that handles real-time collaboration while integrating seamlessly with the existing event-driven infrastructure.\n\n### New Components Required\n\n#### 1. WebSocket Gateway Service\nA dedicated service for handling low-latency, bidirectional communication.\n\n```rust\n// Proposed: whiteboard_gateway/src/lib.rs\npub struct WhiteboardGateway {\n    sessions: Arc<DashMap<SessionId, WhiteboardSession>>,\n    redis: RedisClient,        // For cross-instance state sync\n    auth_service: AuthClient,  // Validate JWT tokens\n    event_bus: NatsEventBus,   // Integration with core event system\n}\n\nimpl WhiteboardGateway {\n    /// Upgrade HTTP to WebSocket connection\n    pub async fn handle_upgrade(\n        &self,\n        ws: WebSocket,\n        session_id: SessionId,\n        user_token: JwtToken,\n    ) -> Result<(), GatewayError>;\n\n    /// Broadcast drawing action to all session participants\n    pub async fn broadcast_action(\n        &self,\n        session_id: SessionId,\n        action: DrawingAction,\n    ) -> Result<(), GatewayError>;\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. CRDT-based State Management\nFor conflict-free real-time collaboration:\n\n```rust\n// Proposed: whiteboard_crdt/src/lib.rs\npub struct WhiteboardState {\n    /// CRDT for concurrent stroke additions\n    strokes: LWWMap<StrokeId, Stroke>,\n    /// Version vector for causality tracking\n    version: VectorClock,\n    /// Participants currently in session\n    participants: HashSet<UserId>,\n}\n```\n\n#### 3. Persistence Strategy\n\n| Data Type | Storage | Rationale |\n|-----------|---------|-----------|\n| Transient drawing actions | Redis Pub/Sub | Sub-millisecond latency for real-time sync |\n| Session state (active) | Redis Hash | Fast read/write for live collaboration |\n| Whiteboard snapshots | PostgreSQL (JSONB) | Durable storage for session recovery |\n| Completed whiteboards | S3/MinIO (SVG/PNG) | Long-term archival as static assets |\n\n### Integration with Existing Systems\n\n#### Authentication Integration (module_1.txt)\n```rust\n// Reuse existing JWT validation\nasync fn validate_websocket_connection(\n    &self,\n    token: &str,\n) -> Result<AuthenticatedUser, AuthError> {\n    // Call existing AuthenticationService\n    self.auth_service.verify_token(token).await\n}\n```\n\n#### Event Bus Integration (module_4.txt, module_12.txt)\n```rust\n// Publish whiteboard lifecycle events to existing event bus\npub enum WhiteboardEvent {\n    SessionStarted { session_id: Uuid, study_group_id: Uuid, started_by: Uuid },\n    SessionEnded { session_id: Uuid, participant_count: u32, duration_secs: u64 },\n    WhiteboardSaved { session_id: Uuid, storage_url: String },\n}\n\nimpl WhiteboardGateway {\n    async fn publish_session_event(&self, event: WhiteboardEvent) {\n        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(event);\n        self.event_bus.publish(envelope).await?;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Study Group Integration\n- Whiteboard sessions are scoped to existing Study Groups\n- Permissions derived from study group membership\n- Events trigger notifications to group members\n\n### Data Flow: Real-time Drawing Action\n\n```\nUser draws stroke\n    │\n    ▼\n[WebSocket Client] ──► [Whiteboard Gateway]\n                              │\n                              ├──► Validate JWT (module_1 integration)\n                              │\n                              ├──► Apply Debounced snapshot to PostgreSQL\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architectural Diagrams\n\n### Diagram 1: Existing Architecture (Create Pulse Flow)\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    subgraph \"Client Layer\"\n Architectural Risks and Mitigations\n\n### Risk 1: WebSocket Session Statefulness and Horizontal Scaling\n\n**Description:**\nUnlike the existing stateless HTTP services, WebSocket connections are inherently stateful. A user's WebSocket connection is tied to a specific server instance. If multiple gateway instances exist (for high availability), participants in the same whiteboard session may be connected to different servers, requiring cross-instance synchronization.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Session affinity required for load balancing\n- Increased complexity for failover handling\n- Potential message loss during server restarts\n- Higher memory usage per instance\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Redis Pub/Sub for Cross-Instance Sync:** Use Redis Pub/Sub channels keyed by session ID to broadcast drawing actions across all gateway instances.\n2. **Sticky Sessions with Health Checks:** Configure load balancer with WebSocket-aware sticky sessions, but implement reconnection logic that can migrate to healthy instances.\n3. **Session State in Redis:** Store authoritative CRDT state in Redis, treating gateway instances as stateless relays. This allows any instance to serve any session.\n4. **Graceful Degradation:** Implement client-side conflict resolution using CRDTs so that temporary network partitions don't cause data loss.\n\n### Risk 2: Real-time Performance vs. Event Bus Latency Mismatch\n\n**Description:**\nThe existing event bus (NATS/RabbitMQ) is designed for eventual consistency with acceptable latencies of 50-500ms. Real-time collaborative drawing requires sub-20ms latency for a smooth user experience. Using the event bus for drawing actions would create a poor user experience.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Perceived lag in collaborative drawing\n- Potential for high message volume overwhelming the event bus\n- Out-of-order stroke rendering causing visual artifacts\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Dual-Path Communication:**\n   - **Hot path (Real-time):** Use direct WebSocket broadcast via Redis Pub/Sub for drawing actions. This bypasses the event bus entirely for transient data.\n   - **Cold path (Persistence):** Use the existing event bus for lifecycle events (SessionStarted, SessionEnded, WhiteboardSaved) that integrate with analytics and audit systems.\n\n2. **Local-First Rendering:** Apply optimistic updates on the client immediately. The client renders strokes locally before confirmation, masking network latency.\n\n3. **Batch Compression:** Instead of publishing each mouse movement, batch coordinates into polyline segments (e.g., every 50ms) to reduce message frequency by 10-20x while maintaining visual fidelity.\n\n### Risk 3: Data Consistency Between Real-time State and Persistent Storage\n\n**Description:**\nThe whiteboard feature introduces a tension between real-time collaboration (ephemeral, in-memory state) and durable persistence (database snapshots). If snapshots are taken too infrequently, a server crash could lose significant user work. If taken too frequently, it creates excessive database load.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Potential data loss on server failure\n- Database write amplification from frequent snapshots\n- Inconsistent state between what users see and what's persisted\n- Recovery complexity after crashes\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Incremental Operation Log:** Instead of full snapshots, persist the CRDT operation log (append-only). This provides point-in-time recovery with minimal I/O.\n\n2. **Tiered Persistence Strategy:**\n   - **Level 1 (1s):** Write operation log to Redis with 1-hour TTL\n   - **Level 2 (30s):** Compress and checkpoint to PostgreSQL\n   - **Level 3 (Session End):** Generate final SVG/PNG and store in S3\n\n3. **Write-Ahead Logging:** Similar to database WAL, log all operations before acknowledgment. On recovery, replay the log to reconstruct state.\n\n4. **Optimistic Acknowledgment:** Acknowledge user actions after Redis write (Level 1), not after PostgreSQL commit. Provide visual feedback for \"auto-saved\" status.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary Finding:** EduPulse Live is a well-architected event-driven platform following CQRS and event-sourcing patterns. The modular design with trait-based abstractions for `EventPublisher`, `Repository`, and `EventBus` enables clean integration of the proposed whiteboard feature.\n\n- **Key Components Identified:**\n  - `module_1.txt` - Authentication & Session Management (JWT + Redis)\n  - `module_4.txt`, `module_9.txt` - Event Publishing Infrastructure (Outbox Pattern + NATS)\n  - `module_33.txt`, `module_81.txt` - Domain Service Layer (Command Handlers)\n  - `module_15.txt`, `module_27.txt` - Event Consumers (Read Model Projections)\n\n- **Architectural Patterns:**\n  - **Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)** - Asynchronous event publishing via NATS/RabbitMQ/Kafka\n  - **CQRS** - Separate write (PostgreSQL) and read (Elasticsearch) models\n  - **Outbox Pattern** - Reliable at-least-once event delivery\n  - **Repository Pattern** - Abstracted data access with async traits\n\n- **Proposed Integration:**\n  - **WebSocket Gateway** for sub-20ms real-time communication\n  - **CRDT-based state management** for conflict-free collaboration\n  - **Dual-path architecture:** Redis Pub/Sub for hot path, existing event bus for cold path\n  - **Tiered persistence:** Redis → PostgreSQL → S3 for reliability/performance balance\n\n- **Critical Risks:**\n  1. Stateful WebSocket scaling → Mitigate with Redis-backed state externalization\n  2. Latency mismatch → Mitigate with dedicated real-time path separate from event bus\n  3. Consistency challenges → Mitigate with operation logging and tiered persistence\n\nThe existing architecture's emphasis on trait-based abstractions and event-driven communication provides an excellent foundation for integrating real-time collaborative features while maintaining the system's reliability and scalability characteristics.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":432,"cache_read_input_tokens":104335,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":432,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d374d4b8-5693-4ece-b4c0-fd35e23c698f","uuid":"ccaaa20d-6982-42b5-a49f-1937a9fa25c3"}
