The platform follows a CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) architecture with an event-sourcing pattern. After thorough analysis of the codebase, I have identified the core architectural components, traced a complete data flow, and developed a strategic integration plan for the proposed Real-time Collaborative Whiteboard feature.\n\n---\n\n## Key Files Identified\n\n### Configuration and Infrastructure\n- `/app/project/src/config.txt` - Application configuration with database, broker, auth, and search settings\n- `/app/project/package.json` - Frontend dependencies including socket.io-client for real-time communications\n\n### Authentication & Session Management\n- `/app/project/src/module_1.txt` - **AuthenticationService** - JWT-based authentication with Redis session management, bcrypt password hashing, and role-based access control\n\n### Event Bus Infrastructure\n- `/app/project/src/module_4.txt` - **EventPublisher & EventStore** - Core event infrastructure using NATS JetStream with event sourcing\n- `/app/project/src/module_9.txt` - **Outbox Pattern Implementation** - Reliable event publishing with Ed25519 signatures and NATS broker\n- `/app/project/src/module_12.txt` - **Event Bus Abstraction** - NATS-backed event bus with in-process fan-out and back-pressure awareness\n- `/app/project/src/module_50.txt` - **Outbox Processor** - RabbitMQ/AMQP event publishing with exponential backoff retries\n\n### Domain Services (Write Model)\n- `/app/project/src/module_33.txt` - **PulseService** - Learning Pulse domain with Service/Repository/EventPublisher pattern\n- `/app/project/src/module_60.txt` - **PulseService (Alternative)** - Business logic for creating learning pulses and handling student replies\n- `/app/project/src/module_81.txt` - **LearningPulseService** - Domain model with validation, event generation, and persistence\n\n### Event Consumers (Read Model / CQRS Projections)\n- `/app/project/src/module_15.txt` - **Badge Awarding Service** - Event consumer that awards badges based on domain events\n- `/app/project/src/module_27.txt` - **Search Indexing Service** - Event consumer that indexes pulses into Elasticsearch\n- `/app/project/src/module_20.txt` - **EventDispatcher** - Combines event store persistence with in-process fan-out\n\n### Event Infrastructure (Multiple Broker Support)\n- `/app/project/src/module_64.txt` - **Kafka EventBus** - Kafka-backed event bus with audit trail consumer\n- `/app/project/src/module_72.txt` - **NATS EventPublisher** - Domain event infrastructure with typed events and NATS publishing\n\n### GDPR & Compliance\n- `/app/project/src/module_45.txt` - **GDPR Erasure Service** - Event-sourced user data erasure with soft-delete events\n- `/app/project/src/module_6.txt` - **Audit Event Store** - GDPR-compliant event store with pseudonymization support\n\n---\n\n## Code Evidence\n\n### 1. Authentication & Session Management (module_1.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_1.txt\n// Lines: 14-27 (Service Description)\n//!\n//! EduPulse Live – Session-based Authentication Service.\n//!\n//! Responsibilities:\n//! ● Secure user registration (bcrypt password hashing)\n//! ● JWT-based authentication with refresh tokens\n//! ● Session storage in Redis with configurable TTL\n//! ● Role-based access control (RBAC)\n//!\n//! The module exposes an HTTP middleware and a standalone service\n//! that can be used from Axum, Actix-web or any other async framework.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_1.txt\n// Lines: 133-157 (JWT Authentication)\npub async fn login(\n    &self,\n    email: &str,\n    password_raw: &str,\n) -> Result<AuthResponse, AuthError> {\n    // 1. Find user by email\n    let user = self\n        .repo\n        .find_by_email(email)\n        .await?\n        .ok_or(AuthError::InvalidCredentials)?;\n\n    // 2. Verify password hash (bcrypt)\n    bcrypt::verify(password_raw, &user.password_hash)?;\n\n    // 3. Mint JWT tokens (access + refresh)\n    let access_token = self.mint_access_token(&user)?;\n    let refresh_token = self.mint_refresh_token(&user)?;\n\n    // 4. Create a server-side session in Redis\n    let session = Session::new(user.id, user.role.clone(), self.session_ttl);\n    self.sessions.set(&session).await?;\n\n    Ok(AuthResponse {\n        access_token,\n        refresh_token,\n        session_id: session.id,\n    })\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Event Publishing Infrastructure (module_4.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_4.txt\n// Lines: 1-20 (Module Description)\n//! src/module_4.rs\n//!\n//! EduPulse Live – Event-Sourcing Core\n//!\n//! This module provides the building blocks for implementing event-sourced\n//! aggregates and reliable message publishing within the \"EduPulse Live\"\n//! platform.  Key components:\n//!\n//! ● `DomainEvent` – trait marking an event eligible for persistence/publishing\n//! ● `EventStore` – trait for persisting events (Postgres implementation)\n//! ● `EventPublisher` – trait for pushing events to the message bus (NATS impl)\n//! ● `OutboxForwarder` – worker that drains the \"outbox\" table and publishes\n//!\n//! The design follows the Outbox Pattern to guarantee *exactly-once* delivery\n//! even when the primary DB and the message broker are separate systems.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_4.txt\n// Lines: 240-268 (NATS JetStream Publisher)\npub struct NatsJsEventPublisher {\n    nc: Arc<nats::asynk::Connection>,\n    jetstream: async_nats::jetstream::Context,\n}\n\nimpl NatsJsEventPublisher {\n    pub async fn connect(url: &str, stream_name: &str) -> Result<Self, PublishError> {\n        let nc = nats::asynk::connect(url)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| PublishError::Connection(e.to_string()))?;\n        let js = async_nats::jetstream::new(nc.clone());\n\n        // Ensure stream exists with sensible defaults\n        js.get_or_create_stream(async_nats::jetstream::stream::Config {\n            name: stream_name.to_string(),\n            subjects: vec![format!(\"{stream_name}.>\")],\n            max_age: Duration::from_secs(60 * 60 * 24 * 7), // 7 days\n            ..Default::default()\n        })\n        .await\n        .map_err(|e| PublishError::JetStream(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            nc: Arc::new(nc),\n            jetstream: js,\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Service Layer Pattern (module_33.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_33.txt\n// Lines: 238-280 (Service Layer)\npub struct PulseService<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> {\n    repo: R,\n    publisher: P,\n    broker_timeout: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl<R: PulseRepository, P: EventPublisher> PulseService<R, P> {\n    /// Create a Pulse and broadcast a `PulseCreated` domain event.\n    ///\n    /// The method is transactional at the application level. If the event\n    /// cannot be published within [`broker_timeout`], the database insert is\n    /// **not** rolled back (best-effort), but the caller is informed through\n    /// an error.\n    pub async fn create_pulse(&self, new: &NewPulse<'_>) -> Result<LearningPulse, PulseError> {\n        let pulse = self.repo.create(new).await?;\n\n        let event = DomainEvent::PulseCreated(PulseCreated {\n            pulse_id: pulse.id,\n            teacher_id: pulse.teacher_id,\n            occurred_at: Utc::now(),\n        });\n\n        // We don't want to await forever; use `tokio::time::timeout`.\n        timeout(self.broker_timeout, self.publisher.publish(&event))\n            .await\n            .map_err(|_| PulseError::Timeout)??;\n\n        Ok(pulse)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Event Consumer Pattern (module_15.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_15.txt\n// Lines: 1-25 (Event Consumer Service)\n//! src/module_15.rs\n//!\n//! Badge-Awarding Worker\n//! ---------------------\n//! This module implements a background worker that listens to domain events and\n//! awards badges to users when they satisfy certain achievement criteria.  It\n//! showcases:\n//!  • Event-driven, fully asynchronous architecture\n//!  • Clean separation of Event-handling / Repository / Service concerns\n//!  • Structured logging via `tracing`\n//!  • Strong typing for event payloads\n//!\n//! In production this worker would be deployed as a separate binary or k8s job,\n//! connected to the message broker (NATS / RabbitMQ / Kafka).  For testability\n//! the broker abstraction is hidden behind an `EventConsumer` trait.\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_15.txt\n// Lines: 180-210 (Event Handling Logic)\nasync fn handle_event(\n    &self,\n    envelope: EventEnvelope<'_>,\n) -> Result<(), WorkerError> {\n    match &envelope.payload {\n        DomainEvent::QuizSubmitted {\n            student_id,\n            score,\n            ..\n        } if *score >= 90.0 => {\n            // Award \"Quiz Ace\" badge\n            let badge = Badge::new(\n                *student_id,\n                BadgeKind::QuizAce,\n                \"Scored ≥90% on a quiz\",\n            );\n            self.badge_repo.save(badge).await?;\n            info!(user_id = %student_id, \"Awarded QuizAce badge\");\n        }\n        DomainEvent::PeerFeedbackGiven {\n            reviewer_id, ..\n        } => {\n            // Award \"Helpful Peer\" badge after 5 feedbacks\n            let count = self.badge_repo.count_by_user(*reviewer_id).await?;\n            if count >= 5 {\n                let badge = Badge::new(*reviewer_id, BadgeKind::HelpfulPeer, \"Gave 5+ peer feedbacks\");\n                self.badge_repo.save(badge).await?;\n            }\n        }\n        _ => {}\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n### 5. CQRS Evidence - Configuration with Multiple DB URLs\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n// Lines: 63-69 (Database Configuration)\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct DatabaseConfig {\n    pub driver: String,\n    pub url: String,\n    pub pool_size: u32,\n}\n```\n\n```yaml\n# File: /app/project/src/config.txt (Example Configuration)\n# Lines: 22-52\ndatabase:\n  driver: \"postgres\"\n  url: \"postgres://epulse:secret@db/edupulse_live\"\n  pool_size: 30\nbroker:\n  host: \"rabbitmq\"\n  port: 5672\n  username: \"mq_user\"\n  password: \"mq_pass\"\n  vhost: \"/edupulse\"\nserver:\n  redis_uri: \"redis://redis:6379\"\n  redis_session_ttl_secs: 86_400\nsearch:\n  elasticsearch_url: \"http://elasticsearch:9200\"\n  index_prefix: \"edupulse_live_\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Core Architectural Components Identification\n\nBased on comprehensive code analysis, the EduPulse Live platform implements the following architectural patterns:\n\n| Component Role | Module(s) | Evidence |\n|---------------|-----------|----------|\n| **Authentication & Session** | `module_1.txt` | JWT tokens, bcrypt hashing, Redis session store, RBAC |\n| **Event Bus / Message Broker** | `module_4.txt`, `module_12.txt`, `module_64.txt`, `module_72.txt` | NATS JetStream, RabbitMQ (lapin), Kafka (rdkafka) implementations |\n| **Command Handlers (Write Model)** | `module_33.txt`, `module_60.txt`, `module_81.txt` | PulseService with Repository + EventPublisher pattern |\n| **Event Consumers (Read Model)** | `module_15.txt`, `module_27.txt`, `module_20.txt` | Badge awarding, Search indexing, Audit trail consumers |\n| **Outbox Pattern** | `module_9.txt`, `module_50.txt` | Reliable at-least-once event delivery with database-backed outbox |\n| **Event Store** | `module_4.txt`, `module_45.txt`, `module_6.txt` | PostgreSQL event store with append-only semantics |\n\n### 2. Architectural Patterns Identified\n\n#### Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)\nThe system uses an event-driven architecture where domain changes are captured as immutable events and published to a message broker for downstream processing.\n\n**Evidence:**\n- Multiple `EventPublisher` trait implementations (NATS, RabbitMQ, Kafka)\n- `DomainEvent` enums with typed event variants (`PulseCreated`, `QuizSubmitted`, `BadgeAwarded`)\n- Event envelope wrappers with correlation IDs and metadata\n- Asynchronous event consumers for search indexing and badge awarding\n\n#### CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation)\nThe system separates write operations (commands) from read operations (queries), with events bridging the two models.\n\n**Evidence:**\n- Write model: `PulseService` handles commands like `create_pulse()` and persists to primary database\n- Read model: `SearchIndexer` (module_27) maintains Elasticsearch index for optimized queries\n- Event-driven synchronization: Events flow from write services to read model consumers\n- Separate infrastructure: PostgreSQL for writes, Elasticsearch for search queries, Redis for sessions\n\n#### Outbox Pattern\nTo ensure reliable event delivery even when the database and message broker are separate systems, the platform uses the Outbox pattern.\n\n**Evidence from module_9.txt:**\n```rust\n/// Outbox service coordinates persistence and dispatching.\npub struct OutboxService<R: OutboxRepository + Send + Sync> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    nats_url: String,\n    key_pair: Arc<Ed25519KeyPair>,\n}\n\n/// Persists a domain event within an existing transaction.\npub async fn stage_event<E: DomainEvent>(\n    &self,\n    tx: &mut Transaction<'_, Postgres>,\n    aggregate_id: Uuid,\n    event: &E,\n) -> Result<(), OutboxError> {\n    // Event is persisted in the same transaction as the domain change\n    self.repo.insert(tx, &unsigned).await\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Data Flow Analysis: Creating a Post/Pulse in a Study Group\n\nBased on the codebase analysis, here is the complete lifecycle of a user creating a new \"Learning Pulse\" (equivalent to a post in a study group):\n\n### Step-by-Step Flow\n\n1. **API Request (HTTP Layer)**\n   - User sends authenticated POST request to create a pulse\n   - JWT token validated by `module_1.txt` (AuthenticationService)\n   - Session verified against Redis\n\n2. **Command Processing (module_33.txt / module_81.txt)**\n   - `PulseService.create_pulse()` receives the command\n   - Domain validation performed (non-empty title, description)\n   - `LearningPulse` aggregate created with new UUID\n\n3. **Persistence (Repository Layer)**\n   - `PgPulseRepository.create()` inserts record into PostgreSQL\n   - Uses SQLx with compile-time query checking\n\n4. **Event Generation**\n   - `PulseCreated` domain event generated with metadata\n   - Event wrapped in `EventEnvelope` with correlation ID\n\n5. **Event Publishing (module_4.txt / module_9.txt)**\n   - Event staged in outbox table within same transaction (Outbox Pattern)\n   - `OutboxProcessor` polls for unpublished events\n   - Event published to NATS/RabbitMQ with routing key `pulse.created`\n\n6. **Event Consumption (module_27.txt / module_15.txt)**\n   - `SearchIndexer` receives `PulsePublished` event\n   - Fetches full pulse data from repository\n   - Indexes document into Elasticsearch for search queries\n   - Badge service checks if user qualifies for achievements\n\n### Sequence of Modules Involved\n\n```\nClient Request\n    │\n    ▼\n[module_1.txt] Authentication/Session Validation\n    │\n    ▼\n[module_33.txt or module_81.txt] PulseService.create_pulse()\n    │\n    ├──▶ [PostgreSQL] Insert learning_pulses record\n    │\n    └──▶ [module_9.txt or module_50.txt] Outbox Event Staging\n              │\n              ▼\n         [module_4.txt or module_12.txt] Event Publication to NATS/RabbitMQ\n              │\n              ├──▶ [module_27.txt] Search Indexer → Elasticsearch\n              │\n              └──▶ [module_15.txt] Badge Worker → Award badges\n```\n\n---\n\n## Proposed Integration Strategy: Real-time Collaborative Whiteboard\n\n### High-Level Architecture\n\nThe proposed architecture introduces a dedicated **Whiteboard Service** that handles real-time collaboration while integrating seamlessly with the existing event-driven infrastructure.\n\n### New Components Required\n\n#### 1. WebSocket Gateway Service\nA dedicated service for handling low-latency, bidirectional communication.\n\n```rust\n// Proposed: whiteboard_gateway/src/lib.rs\npub struct WhiteboardGateway {\n    sessions: Arc<DashMap<SessionId, WhiteboardSession>>,\n    redis: RedisClient,        // For cross-instance state sync\n    auth_service: AuthClient,  // Validate JWT tokens\n    event_bus: NatsEventBus,   // Integration with core event system\n}\n\nimpl WhiteboardGateway {\n    /// Upgrade HTTP to WebSocket connection\n    pub async fn handle_upgrade(\n        &self,\n        ws: WebSocket,\n        session_id: SessionId,\n        user_token: JwtToken,\n    ) -> Result<(), GatewayError>;\n\n    /// Broadcast drawing action to all session participants\n    pub async fn broadcast_action(\n        &self,\n        session_id: SessionId,\n        action: DrawingAction,\n    ) -> Result<(), GatewayError>;\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. CRDT-based State Management\nFor conflict-free real-time collaboration:\n\n```rust\n// Proposed: whiteboard_crdt/src/lib.rs\npub struct WhiteboardState {\n    /// CRDT for concurrent stroke additions\n    strokes: LWWMap<StrokeId, Stroke>,\n    /// Version vector for causality tracking\n    version: VectorClock,\n    /// Participants currently in session\n    participants: HashSet<UserId>,\n}\n```\n\n#### 3. Persistence Strategy\n\n| Data Type | Storage | Rationale |\n|-----------|---------|-----------|\n| Transient drawing actions | Redis Pub/Sub | Sub-millisecond latency for real-time sync |\n| Session state (active) | Redis Hash | Fast read/write for live collaboration |\n| Whiteboard snapshots | PostgreSQL (JSONB) | Durable storage for session recovery |\n| Completed whiteboards | S3/MinIO (SVG/PNG) | Long-term archival as static assets |\n\n### Integration with Existing Systems\n\n#### Authentication Integration (module_1.txt)\n```rust\n// Reuse existing JWT validation\nasync fn validate_websocket_connection(\n    &self,\n    token: &str,\n) -> Result<AuthenticatedUser, AuthError> {\n    // Call existing AuthenticationService\n    self.auth_service.verify_token(token).await\n}\n```\n\n#### Event Bus Integration (module_4.txt, module_12.txt)\n```rust\n// Publish whiteboard lifecycle events to existing event bus\npub enum WhiteboardEvent {\n    SessionStarted { session_id: Uuid, study_group_id: Uuid, started_by: Uuid },\n    SessionEnded { session_id: Uuid, participant_count: u32, duration_secs: u64 },\n    WhiteboardSaved { session_id: Uuid, storage_url: String },\n}\n\nimpl WhiteboardGateway {\n    async fn publish_session_event(&self, event: WhiteboardEvent) {\n        let envelope = EventEnvelope::new(event);\n        self.event_bus.publish(envelope).await?;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### Study Group Integration\n- Whiteboard sessions are scoped to existing Study Groups\n- Permissions derived from study group membership\n- Events trigger notifications to group members\n\n### Data Flow: Real-time Drawing Action\n\n```\nUser draws stroke\n    │\n    ▼\n[WebSocket Client] ──► [Whiteboard Gateway]\n                              │\n                              ├──► Validate JWT (module_1 integration)\n                              │\n                              ├──► Apply Debounced snapshot to PostgreSQL\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architectural Diagrams\n\n### Diagram 1: Existing Architecture (Create Pulse Flow)\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n    subgraph \"Client Layer\"\n Architectural Risks and Mitigations\n\n### Risk 1: WebSocket Session Statefulness and Horizontal Scaling\n\n**Description:**\nUnlike the existing stateless HTTP services, WebSocket connections are inherently stateful. A user's WebSocket connection is tied to a specific server instance. If multiple gateway instances exist (for high availability), participants in the same whiteboard session may be connected to different servers, requiring cross-instance synchronization.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Session affinity required for load balancing\n- Increased complexity for failover handling\n- Potential message loss during server restarts\n- Higher memory usage per instance\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Redis Pub/Sub for Cross-Instance Sync:** Use Redis Pub/Sub channels keyed by session ID to broadcast drawing actions across all gateway instances.\n2. **Sticky Sessions with Health Checks:** Configure load balancer with WebSocket-aware sticky sessions, but implement reconnection logic that can migrate to healthy instances.\n3. **Session State in Redis:** Store authoritative CRDT state in Redis, treating gateway instances as stateless relays. This allows any instance to serve any session.\n4. **Graceful Degradation:** Implement client-side conflict resolution using CRDTs so that temporary network partitions don't cause data loss.\n\n### Risk 2: Real-time Performance vs. Event Bus Latency Mismatch\n\n**Description:**\nThe existing event bus (NATS/RabbitMQ) is designed for eventual consistency with acceptable latencies of 50-500ms. Real-time collaborative drawing requires sub-20ms latency for a smooth user experience. Using the event bus for drawing actions would create a poor user experience.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Perceived lag in collaborative drawing\n- Potential for high message volume overwhelming the event bus\n- Out-of-order stroke rendering causing visual artifacts\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Dual-Path Communication:**\n   - **Hot path (Real-time):** Use direct WebSocket broadcast via Redis Pub/Sub for drawing actions. This bypasses the event bus entirely for transient data.\n   - **Cold path (Persistence):** Use the existing event bus for lifecycle events (SessionStarted, SessionEnded, WhiteboardSaved) that integrate with analytics and audit systems.\n\n2. **Local-First Rendering:** Apply optimistic updates on the client immediately. The client renders strokes locally before confirmation, masking network latency.\n\n3. **Batch Compression:** Instead of publishing each mouse movement, batch coordinates into polyline segments (e.g., every 50ms) to reduce message frequency by 10-20x while maintaining visual fidelity.\n\n### Risk 3: Data Consistency Between Real-time State and Persistent Storage\n\n**Description:**\nThe whiteboard feature introduces a tension between real-time collaboration (ephemeral, in-memory state) and durable persistence (database snapshots). If snapshots are taken too infrequently, a server crash could lose significant user work. If taken too frequently, it creates excessive database load.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Potential data loss on server failure\n- Database write amplification from frequent snapshots\n- Inconsistent state between what users see and what's persisted\n- Recovery complexity after crashes\n\n**Mitigation:**\n1. **Incremental Operation Log:** Instead of full snapshots, persist the CRDT operation log (append-only). This provides point-in-time recovery with minimal I/O.\n\n2. **Tiered Persistence Strategy:**\n   - **Level 1 (1s):** Write operation log to Redis with 1-hour TTL\n   - **Level 2 (30s):** Compress and checkpoint to PostgreSQL\n   - **Level 3 (Session End):** Generate final SVG/PNG and store in S3\n\n3. **Write-Ahead Logging:** Similar to database WAL, log all operations before acknowledgment. On recovery, replay the log to reconstruct state.\n\n4. **Optimistic Acknowledgment:** Acknowledge user actions after Redis write (Level 1), not after PostgreSQL commit. Provide visual feedback for \"auto-saved\" status.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary Finding:** EduPulse Live is a well-architected event-driven platform following CQRS and event-sourcing patterns. The modular design with trait-based abstractions for `EventPublisher`, `Repository`, and `EventBus` enables clean integration of the proposed whiteboard feature.\n\n- **Key Components Identified:**\n  - `module_1.txt` - Authentication & Session Management (JWT + Redis)\n  - `module_4.txt`, `module_9.txt` - Event Publishing Infrastructure (Outbox Pattern + NATS)\n  - `module_33.txt`, `module_81.txt` - Domain Service Layer (Command Handlers)\n  - `module_15.txt`, `module_27.txt` - Event Consumers (Read Model Projections)\n\n- **Architectural Patterns:**\n  - **Event-Driven Architecture (EDA)** - Asynchronous event publishing via NATS/RabbitMQ/Kafka\n  - **CQRS** - Separate write (PostgreSQL) and read (Elasticsearch) models\n  - **Outbox Pattern** - Reliable at-least-once event delivery\n  - **Repository Pattern** - Abstracted data access with async traits\n\n- **Proposed Integration:**\n  - **WebSocket Gateway** for sub-20ms real-time communication\n  - **CRDT-based state management** for conflict-free collaboration\n  - **Dual-path architecture:** Redis Pub/Sub for hot path, existing event bus for cold path\n  - **Tiered persistence:** Redis → PostgreSQL → S3 for reliability/performance balance\n\n- **Critical Risks:**\n  1. Stateful WebSocket scaling → Mitigate with Redis-backed state externalization\n  2. Latency mismatch → Mitigate with dedicated real-time path separate from event bus\n  3. Consistency challenges → Mitigate with operation logging and tiered persistence\n\nThe existing architecture's emphasis on trait-based abstractions and event-driven communication provides an excellent foundation for integrating real-time collaborative features while maintaining the system's reliability and scalability characteristics.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

`module_1.txt` - Authentication & Session Management (JWT + Redis)\n   - `module_4.txt`, `module_9.txt` - Event Publishing Infrastructure\n   - `module_33.txt`, `module_81.txt` - Domain Service Layer (Command Handlers)\n   - `module_15.txt`, `module_27.txt` - Event Consumers (Read Model Projections)\n\n3. **Data Flow Traced:**\n   - Complete lifecycle from HTTP request → Auth validation → Command handling → Event publishing → Consumer processing\n\n4. **Whiteboard Integration Proposal:**\n   - WebSocket Gateway for real-time communication (<20ms latency)\n   - CRDT-based state management for conflict-free collaboration\n   - Dual-path architecture: Redis Pub/Sub (hot path) + Event Bus (cold path)\n   -
